Secondly, we have $\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\equiv(\mathbb{C}_1+\mathbb{L}^\ell)'\mathbb{PC}_2=\mathbb{C}_1'\mathbb{PC}_2+\mathbb{C}_2'\mathbb{PL}^\ell$. Similarly, we have
$$\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\frac{1}{NT}E\left[\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\right]=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)$$
and $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\right)=O(1)$ by Lemmas \ref{lm_CQL} and \ref{lm_lag}, which also holds for $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_1$, $\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_4$, $\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_1$, $\mathbb{Z}_2'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$, $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_2$, $\mathbb{Z}_2'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_4$ and $\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_2$.


Lastly, the scalar $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$, as well as each element of the vector $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_4$, $\mathbb{Z}_4\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ and the matrix $\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_4$ is of the form $\mathbb{C}_1'\mathbb{PC}_2$ and $\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{C}_1'\mathbb{PC}_2=O(1)$.
Therefore, equation (\ref{eq_ZPZ}) follows and $\frac{1}{NT}E[\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}]=O(1)$.
\end{proof}



\begin{lemma}\label{lm_Lambda}
The results in Lemmas \ref{lm15} and \ref{lm_ZPZ} still hold when $\mathbb{Z}$ is replaced by $\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}$, where $\widetilde{\Gamma}=\mathrm{blkdiag}(I_2,\sqrt{T},I_k)$. Furthermore, 
\begin{align*}
&\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O_p\left(N\sqrt{T}\right) & T\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O(NT).
\end{align*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For equations (\ref{eq_ZBV}) and (\ref{eq_ZPV}) with $\mathbb{Z}$ replaced by $\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}$, we only need to further show that 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{V}=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)
&\text{and\qquad\quad}
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{V}=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\end{align*}
which have been proved in Lemma \ref{lm_Z_gamma}.

For equations (\ref{eq_ZBZ}) and (\ref{eq_ZPZ}) with $\mathbb{Z}$ replaced by $\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}$, since $\mathbb{Z}_1$ and $\mathbb{Z}_2$ take the same form and $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ and $\mathbb{Z}_4$ are nonstochastic, we need to further show that 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(1\right),
&\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(1\right),\\
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),\\
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\end{align*}

Firstly, we can prove $\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(1\right)$ and
$\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(1\right)$ because $\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2$ and $\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2$ are UB and $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ is a sparse binary vector with at most $N$ elements.

Secondly, since elements in $\mathbb{Z}_4$ are uniformly bounded, $\|\mathbb{Z}_4\|_{\infty}=O(1)$,
$\|\mathbb{B}_2'\mathbb{QB}_1\mathbb{Z}_4\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbb{B}_2'\mathbb{QB}_1\|_{\infty}\|\mathbb{Z}_4\|_{\infty}=O(1)$, which means that elements in $\mathbb{B}_2'\mathbb{QB}_1\mathbb{Z}_4$ are also uniformly bounded. Therefore, $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$ by sparsity of $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ and we can similarly prove $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$.

Lastly, $\mathbb{B}_1\mathbb{Z}_1$ can be viewed as $\mathbb{L}^\ell+\mathbb{C}$, where $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$ and $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{C}\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$. Moreover, we have $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)$ and $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)$ by Lemma \ref{lm_Z_gamma}. Taken together, we can prove 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),\\
&\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\end{align*}
and $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$, $\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}E(\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{PB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)})=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$.

The above proof also indicates that $\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O_p\left(N\sqrt{T}\right)$ and $ T\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_1'\mathbb{QB}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O(NT)$.
\end{proof}

Based on the definition of $\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*$ in Section 3.2, the following lemma will be used in Appendix \ref{B2} to show that the expectation of the Hessian matrix and the corresponding part of the variance of the score function are asymptotically equivalent up to an $O(1/T)$ remainder.
\begin{lemma}\label{lm_Z*}
Under Assumptions 1-7 and \ref{asp_A2}, we have
\begin{gather}
\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)\label{eq_Z*Z}\\
\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QG}_0\mathbb{Z}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)\label{eq_Z*GZ}\\
\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\mathbb{Z'G}_0'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)\label{eq_GZ*GZ}
\end{gather}
where $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)$, $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)$ and $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)$ are all $O(1)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Recall that $\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}=\mathbb{QZ+PZ}^\circ$,
$$\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)-\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z^{\circ\prime}PZ}^\circ\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\mathbb{Z^{\circ\prime}PZ}^\circ\right),$$
where the last equality holds by $\mathbb{Z}^\circ=(Z^{\circ\prime}_1,\cdots,Z^{\circ\prime}_T)'$ and $Z^\circ_t=\bigl(D_t(I_N-F_t)D_t'M_t\mathcal{V}_{t-1},\allowbreak D_tD_{t-1}'\mathcal{V}_{t-1},\allowbreak\mathbf{0}_{N_t\times(k+1)}\bigr)$. Since $\mathbb{Z}^\circ$ takes the form of $(\mathbb{L}^\ell,\mathbb{R}^\ell,\mathbf{0}_{n\times(k+1)})$, we have $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\mathbb{Z^{\circ\prime}PZ}^\circ\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)$ by Lemma \ref{lm_PPQ}. Additionally, we have $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=O(1)$ by Lemmas \ref{lm15} and \ref{lm_Lambda}. Thus, equation (\ref{eq_Z*Z}) holds and $\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=O(1)$.

Equations (\ref{eq_Z*GZ}) and (\ref{eq_GZ*GZ}) can be proved analogously by the aforementioned method.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{lm_EL4}
Under Assumptions 2 and \ref{asp_A2}, $E\left[\left(L_t^\ell\right)_i^4\right]=O(1)$ uniformly for $i,t,N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Denote $e_i$ as an $N_t$ dimensional vector where the $i$-th component equals 1 and all other components are 0. For simplicity, let $B_t^\ell\equiv B_{1t}^\ell B_{1,t-1}$. Thus, $(L_t^\ell)_i=\sum_{h=0}^\infty e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}V_{t-1-h}$. Then using Lemma \ref{lm_quadratic form}, we have
\begin{align*}
&E\left[\left(L_t^\ell\right)_i^4\right]\\
=&E\left[\left(\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}V_{t-1-h_1}\right)'\left(\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}V_{t-1-h_2}\right)\left(\sum_{h_3=0}^\infty e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_3)}V_{t-1-h_3}\right)'\right.\\
&\left.\left(\sum_{h_4=0}^\infty e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_4)}V_{t-1-h_4}\right)\right]\\%\sum_{h_1,h_2,h_3,h_4=0}^\infty
=&\sum_{h_1,h_2,h_3,h_4=0}^\infty E\left[\left(V_{t-1-h_1}'P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}V_{t-1-h_2}\right)\left(V_{t-1-h_3}'P_{t-1}^{(h_3)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_4)}V_{t-1-h_4}\right)\right]\\
=&\sum_{h=0}^\infty(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{N_{t-1-h}}\left[\left(P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}\right)_{jj}\right]^2+\\
&\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}\left(P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}\right)\mathrm{tr}\left(P_{t-1}^{(h_2)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}\right)+\\
&\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}\left[P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}\left(P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}\right)'\right]+\\
&\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}\left[P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}\left(P_{t-1}^{(h_2)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}\right)'\right]\\
=&\sum_{h=0}^\infty(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{N_{t-1-h}}\left[\left(P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}\right)_{jj}\right]^2+\\
&\sigma_0^4\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\left[\left(e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_i\right)\left(e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_2)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_i\right)+\right.\\
&\left.\left(e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_2)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_i\right)+\left(e_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_ie_i'B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}e_i\right)\right]\\
=&\sum_{h=0}^\infty(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{N_{t-1-h}}\left[\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}\right)_{ij}\right]^4+2\sigma_0^4\left[\sum_{h=0}^\infty\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)_{ii}\right]^2+\\
&\sigma_0^4\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\left[\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)_{ii}\right]^2.
\end{align*}
 
\begin{align*}
\sum_{h=0}^\infty(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{N_{t-1-h}}\left[\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}\right)_{ij}\right]^4&=\sum_{h=0}^\infty(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left[\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}D_{t-1-h}\right)_{ij}\right]^4\\
&\leq(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\left[\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{h=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}D_{t-1-h}\right)_{ij}\right]^4\\
&=O(1),
\end{align*}
where the last equality holds because matrix $\sum_{h=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}D_{t-1-h}\right)$ is UB uniformly for $t$ according to Lemma \ref{lm33}. Moreover,
\begin{align*}
\left[\sum_{h=0}^\infty\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)_{ii}\right]^2\leq\left\{\left[\sum_{h=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)\right]_{ii}\right\}^2=O(1),
\end{align*}
where the equality holds because $\sum_{h=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}P_{t-1}^{(h)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)$ is UB according to Lemma \ref{lm33}. Similarly,
\begin{align*}
&\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\left[\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)_{ii}\right]^2\leq\left\{\left[\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}D_{t-1-h}D_{t-1-h}'P_{t-1}^{(h_1)\prime}B_{t}^{\ell\prime}\right)\right]_{ii}\right\}^2\\
\leq&\left[\left(\sum_{h_2=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_2)}D_{t-1-h}\right)\right)\left(\sum_{h_1=0}^\infty\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h_1)}D_{t-1-h}\right)\right)\right]_{ii}^2=O(1),
\end{align*}
where the equality holds because $\mathrm{abs}\left(B_{t}^\ell P_{t-1}^{(h)}D_{t-1-h}\right)$ is UB uniformly for $t$ according to Lemma \ref{lm33}. Therefore, $E\left[\left(L_t^\ell\right)_i^4\right]=O(1)$.
\end{proof}




%
%the zero-expectation part of the gradient
To establish the central limit theorem for $\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}$, let 
\begin{align}
\mathcal{Q}_n&\equiv\sum_{t=1}^T\left[V_t'B_{3t}V_t+V_t'L_t^\ell+V_t'\widetilde{C}_t-\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t})\right]\nonumber\\
&=\sum_{t=1}^T\left[V_t'B_{3t}V_t+V_t'B_{1t}^\ell L_{t-1}+V_t'\left(C_{t}+a\sqrt{T}D_tF_t\iota_N\right)-\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t})\right]\equiv\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti},\label{eq_clt}
\end{align}
%
where $B_{3t}$ is an arbitrary symmetric\footnote{The symmetry of $B_3$ can be imposed without loss of generality because $V_t'B_{3t}V_t=V_t'[(B_{3t}+B_{3t}')/2]V_t$.} UB matrix uniformly for $t$. The $N_t$-dimensional vector $\widetilde{C}_t\equiv C_{t}+a\sqrt{T}D_tF_t\iota_N$, where $C_{t}$ is an arbitrary vector satisfying Assumption \ref{asp_C}, and $a$ is a constant.
%and $C_t^{(\gamma)}$ is sparse vector such that, over all $t = 1, \cdots, T$, it contains at most $N$ nonzero elements, each of which is of order $O(\sqrt{T})$.
Furthermore, $B_{1t}^\ell$ is an arbitrary matrix satisfying Assumption \ref{asp_A2}.
%There exist constants $c_B>0$ and $c_C>0$ such that 
%$C_t^{(\gamma)}$ is $N_t$-dimensional vector whose elements are bounded, except for a subset of elements that are $O(\sqrt{T})$, however, the total number of such exceptional elements across all $t=1,\cdots, T$ does not exceed $N$.
$\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}\equiv2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}V_{t,i}+[(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i]V_{t,i}+(B_{3t})_{ii}(V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2)$, and $V_{t,i}$ is the $i$-th element in $V_t$. 
Linear combinations of terms satisfying equation (\ref{eq_clt}) can still be written in the same form and do not violate the original assumptions, because 
\begin{align*}
&w^{(1)}\mathcal{Q}_n^{(1)}+w^{(2)}\mathcal{Q}_n^{(2)}\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T\left[V_t'\left(w^{(1)}B_{3t}^{(1)}+w^{(2)}B_{3t}^{(2)}\right)V_t+V_t'\left(w^{(1)}B_{1t}^{\ell(1)}+w^{(2)}B_{1t}^{\ell(2)}\right) L_{t-1}+\right.\\
&\left.V_t'\left(w^{(1)}C_t^{(1)}+w^{(2)}C_t^{(2)}+(w^{(1)}a^{(1)}+w^{(2)}a^{(2)})\sqrt{T}D_tF_t\iota_N\right)-\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}\left(w^{(1)}B_{3t}^{(1)}+w^{(2)}B_{3t}^{(2)}\right)\right].
\end{align*}
Then we have $E(\mathcal{Q}_n)=0$ and the variance is
\begin{align*}
\sigma^2_{\mathcal{Q}_n}=&E\left[\left(\sum_{t=1}^TV_t'B_{3t}V_t+V_t'L_t^\ell+V_t'\widetilde{C}_t-\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t})\right)^2\right]\\
=&E\Bigg[\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{s=1}^T\left(V_t'B_{3t}V_tV_s'B_{3s}V_s+2V_t'B_{3t}V_tV_s'(L_s^\ell+\widetilde{C}_s)-2V_t'B_{3t}V_t\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}(B_{3s})+\right.\\
&\left. V_t'(L_t^\ell+\widetilde{C}_t)V_s'(L_s^\ell+\widetilde{C}_s)-2V_t'(L_t^\ell+\widetilde{C}_t)\sigma_0^2\mathrm{tr}(B_{3s})+\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t})\mathrm{tr}(B_{3s})\right)\Bigg]\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T\left[(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}^2+2\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t}^2)\right]+2\mu_3\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}(\widetilde{C}_t)_i+\\
&\sigma_0^4\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{h=0}^\infty\mathrm{tr}(B_{1t}^\ell P_{t-1,h}P_{t-1,h}'B_{1t}^{\ell\prime})+\sigma_0^2\sum_{t=1}^T\widetilde{C}_t'\widetilde{C}_t\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T\left[(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}^2+2\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t}^2)\right]+2\mu_3\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}\left[(C_t)_i+a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i\right]+\\
&+\sigma_0^4\sum_{t=1}^T\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{1t}^\ell\sum_{h=0}^\infty\left(P_{t-1,h}P_{t-1,h}'\right)B_{1t}^{\ell\prime}\right]+\sigma_0^2\sum_{t=1}^T\left(C_t+a\sqrt{T}D_tF_t\iota_N\right)'\left(C_t+a\sqrt{T}D_tF_t\iota_N\right)\\
%\leq&(\mu_4-\sigma_0^4)c_B^2NT+\sigma_0^4\sum_{t=1}^T\mathrm{tr}\left[B_{1t}^\ell\sum_{h=0}^\infty\left(P_{t-1,h}P_{t-1,h}'\right)B_{1t}^{\ell\prime}\right]+\\
%&2\mu_3c_Bc_CNT+2\mu_3c_Bc_CN\sqrt{T}+\sigma_0^2c_C^2NT+2\sigma_0^2c_C^2N\sqrt{T}+\sigma_0^2c_C^2NT\\
=&O(NT),
\end{align*}
where the last equality holds because $B_{3t}$ is UB uniformly for $t$, elements in $C_t$ are uniformly bounded, $F_t\iota_N$ is a sparse vector with fewer than $N$ nonzero entries in total over $t=1,\cdots,T$, and the matrix inside the trace operation is UB by Lemma \ref{lm33}.
%\textcolor{red}{where last equality holds due to Assumptions \ref{asp_A2}-\ref{asp_C} and Lemma \ref{lm33}, and $B_{3t}$ is UB uniformly for $t$.}
%Moreover, $\sigma_{\mathcal{Q}_n}^2$ is nonnegligible at the $O(NT)$ scale.


\begin{lemma}\label{lm_CLT}
Under Assumptions 2, 7, \ref{asp_A2} and \ref{asp_C}, if the sequence $\frac{1}{n}\sigma^2_{\mathcal{Q}_n}$ is bounded away from zero, then $\frac{\mathcal{Q}_n}{\sigma_{\mathcal{Q}_n}}\overset{d}{\longrightarrow}N(0,1)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The proof of central limit theorem follows the idea in Yu et al. (2008). Denote the $\sigma$-field
\begin{align*}
\mathcal{F}_{n,ti}=\sigma\left(\left( \bigcup_{s=1}^{\infty}\left\{V_{t-s,j}\mid j=1,2,\cdots,N_{t-s}\right\}\right)\cup\left\{V_{t,j}\mid j=1,2,\cdots,i\right\}\right), \\
\text{ for }t=1,2\cdots,T\text{, and }i=1,2,\cdots,N_t,
\end{align*}
%\begin{align*}
%\mathcal{F}_{n,ti}=\sigma\left(\left( \bigcup_{s=1}^{t-1}\left\{v_{js}\mid j\in \mathbb{I}_s\right\}\right)\cup\left\{v_{jt}\mid j\in\{j_1,j_2,\cdots,j_i\}\subset\mathbb{I}_t\right\}\right), \\
%\text{ for }t=1,2\cdots,T\text{, and }i=1,2,\cdots,N_t,
%\end{align*}
which is generated by the $i$-th node's error observed at time $t$ and all its historical errors, where ``historical" includes both errors from all time periods preceding $t$ and those within period $t$ that are ordered before the $i$-th node. Define $\mathcal{F}_{n,t0}=\mathcal{F}_{n,t-1,N_{t-1}}$.
%Define $\mathcal{F}_{n,t,0}=\mathcal{F}_{n,t-1,N_t}$, then $E(q_{n,ti}|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1})=0$, which forms a martingale difference array.
To merge the two indices $t$ and $i$ into a single index, define a global index $j=\sum_{s=1}^{t-1} N_s+i$, where $j$ ranges from $1$ to $n$. The $\sigma$-field $\mathcal{F}_{n,ti}$ is then simplified as $\mathcal{F}_{n,j}$, and $\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}$ is simplified as $\mathfrak{q}_{n,j}$.
Thus, $E(\mathfrak{q}_{n,j}|\mathcal{F}_{n,j-1})=0$,
which forms a martingale difference array. To apply the martingale CLT to $\sum_{j=1}^n\mathfrak{q}_{n,j}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}$, the sufficient conditions are (i) $\frac{1}{\sigma^{2+\eta}_{\mathcal{Q}_n}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}|\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}|^{2+\eta}\rightarrow0$ and (ii) $\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}_{\mathcal{Q}_n}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E(\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}^2|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1})\overset{p}{\rightarrow}1$.

\textbf{Proof of (i)}

For any $p>0$, $q>0$ with $\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1$, we have
\begin{align*}
&|\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}|\\
\leq&\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|+\left|(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right|\right)|V_{t,i}|+|(B_{3t})_{ii}|^{\frac{1}{p}}|(B_{3t})_{ii}|^{\frac{1}{q}}\left|V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|(B_{3t})_{ij}|^{\frac{1}{p}}\left(2|(B_{3t})_{ij}|^{\frac{1}{q}}|V_{t,j}||V_{t,i}|\right)\\
\leq&\left[\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|+\left|(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right|\right)^p+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\left|(B_{3t})_{ij}\right|\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}\left[|V_{t,i}|^{q}+\left|(B_{3t})_{ii}\right|\left|V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2\right|^q+\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}2^q|(B_{3t})_{ij}||V_{t,j}|^q|V_{t,i}|^q\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}
\end{align*}
where the second inequality is derived by applying Hölder's inequality. Thus,
\begin{align*}
E\left[|\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}|^q\right]\leq&E\left[\left(\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|+\left|(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right|\right)^p+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\left|(B_{3t})_{ij}\right|\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}\right]\times\\
&\left[E\left(|V_{t,i}|^{q}\right)+\left|(B_{3t})_{ii}\right|E\left(\left|V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2\right|^q\right)+2^qE\left(|V_{t,i}|^q\right)\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|(B_{3t})_{ij}|E\left(|V_{t,j}|^q\right)\right],
\end{align*}
by the independence between $V_t$ and $L_t^\ell$.
Let $q=2+\eta$ with a small $\eta$ defined in Assumption 2, then $p=\frac{2+\eta}{1+\eta}$. There exists $c_1>0$ such that 
$$\sup_{t,i}\left[E|V_{t,i}|^{q}+|(B_{3t})_{ii}|E\left|V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2\right|^q+2^qE|V_{t,i}|^q\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|(B_{3t})_{ij}|E|V_{t,j}|^q\right]<c_1,$$ due to  $E|v_{it}|^{4+2\eta}<\infty$ by Assumption 2 and $B_{3t}$ is UB uniformly for $t$. Therefore, 
\begin{align*}
&\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E|\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}|^{2+\eta}\\
\leq&c_1\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E\left[\left(\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|+\left|(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right|\right)^p+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\left|(B_{3t})_{ij}\right|\right)^{\frac{q}{p}}\right]\\
\leq&2^\eta c_1\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E\left[\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|+\left|(C_t)_i\right|+\left|a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i\right|\right)^{2+\eta}\right]+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}2^\eta c_1\left(\sum_{j=1}^{i}\left|(B_{3t})_{ij}\right|\right)^{1+\eta}\\
\leq&2^{1+2\eta}c_1\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}
\left[E\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|^{2+\eta}\right)+\left|(C_t)_i\right|^{2+\eta}+T^{1+\frac{\eta}{2}}\left|a(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i\right|^{2+\eta}+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|(B_{3t})_{ij}\right|\right)^{1+\eta}\right]\\
=&O\left(NT^{1+\frac{\eta}{2}}\right),
\end{align*}
where the last two inequalities are derived by applying the $C_r$-inequality.
By Lemma \ref{lm_EL4}, which establishes $E\left(\left|(L_t^\ell)_i\right|^{4}\right) = O(1)$ uniformly for $t$ and $i$, combined with the boundedness of $C_t$, the sparsity of $F_t\iota_N$, and $B_{3t}$ being UB uniformly for $t$, the last equality follows.
Since the sequence $\frac{1}{n}\sigma^2_{\mathcal{Q}_n}$ is bounded away from zero, then $\sigma^{2+\eta}_{\mathcal{Q}_n}=O\left((NT)^{1+\frac{\eta}{2}}\right)$ and it is nonnegligible at this order, we have $\frac{1}{\sigma^{2+\eta}_{\mathcal{Q}_n}}\sum_{j=1}^nE|\mathfrak{q}_{n,j}|^{2+\eta}=O\left(N^{-\frac{\eta}{2}}\right)$, which proves condition (i).

\textbf{Proof of (ii)}

Since
\begin{align*}
E\left(\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}^2|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1}\right)=&\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right]^2E(V_{t,i}^2)+(B_{3t})_{ii}^2E\left[(V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2)^2\right]+\\
&2\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right](B_{3t})_{ii}E\left[V_{t,i}(V_{t,i}^2-\sigma_0^2)\right]\\
=&\sigma_0^2\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right]^2+(\mu_4-\sigma_0^4)(B_{3t})_{ii}^2+\\
&2\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right].
\end{align*}
Then
\begin{align*}
&\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E\left(\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}^2|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\sigma_0^2\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right]^2+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(\mu_4-\sigma_0^4)(B_{3t})_{ii}^2\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}2\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}\left[2\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}+(L_t^\ell)_i+(\widetilde{C}_t)_i\right]\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}4\sigma_0^2\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]^2+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\sigma_0^2(L_t^\ell)_i^2+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\sigma_0^2(\widetilde{C}_t)_i^2\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\left[4\sigma_0^2(C_t)_i+4\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}\right]\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}[2\sigma_0^2(C_t)_i+2\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}](L_t^\ell)_i\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}4a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}2a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i(L_t^\ell)_i\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}4\sigma_0^2\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right](L_t^\ell)_i+\sum_{t=1}^T(\mu_4-\sigma_0^4)\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}^2+2\mu_3\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}(\widetilde{C}_t)_i.
\end{align*}



Denote $\mathbb{B}_{3}\equiv$ $\mathrm{blkdiag}(B_{31},$ $B_{32},\cdots,B_{3T})$, then
$$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]^2=\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}V_t'B_{3t}^{-\prime}B_{3t}^{-}V_t=\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{B}_{3}^{-\prime}\mathbb{B}_{3}^-\mathbb{V},$$ thus by equation (\ref{eq_vbv}) from Lemma \ref{lm_VQV},
$$\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{B}_{3}^{-\prime}\mathbb{B}_{3}^-\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{NT}E(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{B}_{3}^{-\prime}\mathbb{B}_{3}^-\mathbb{V})=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),$$
where $E(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{B}_{3}^{-\prime}\mathbb{B}_{3}^-\mathbb{V})=\sigma_0^2\sum_{t=1}^T\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t}^{-\prime}B_{3t}^{-})$.
Additionally, $\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\sigma_0^2(L_t^\ell)_i^2=\sigma_0^2\sum_{t=1}^TL_t^{\ell\prime}L_t^\ell=\sigma_0^2\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{L}^\ell$, according to equation (\ref{eq_lr}) and Lemma \ref{lm_lag},
$$\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{L}^\ell-\frac{1}{NT}E(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{L}^\ell)=O_P\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),$$
where $E(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{L}^\ell)=\sigma_0^2\sum_{t=1}^T\mathrm{tr}(\sum_{h=0}^\infty B_{1t}^\ell P_{t-1,h}P_{t-1,h}'B_{1t}^{\ell\prime})$.
Denote $C_{2t}$ as an $N_t$ dimensional vector such that elements in $C_{2t}$ are uniformly bounded and $(C_{2t})_i=4\sigma_0^2(C_t)_i+4\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}$, then $$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\left[4\sigma_0^2(C_t)_i+4\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}\right]\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]=\sum_{t=1}^TC_{2t}'B_{3t}^-V_t=O_p\left(\sqrt{NT}\right)$$ by equation (\ref{eq_cv}) in Lemma \ref{lm_CQL}.
Similarly, 
$$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}[2\sigma_0^2(C_t)_i+2\mu_3(B_{3t})_{ii}](L_t^\ell)_i=\sum_{t=1}^TC_{2t}'L_t^\ell=O_p\left(\sqrt{NT}\right)$$
by equation (\ref{eq_cl}) from Lemma \ref{lm_CQL}. Moreover 
$$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right]=\sum_{t=1}^Ta\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)'B_{3t}^-V_t=a\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{B}_3^-\mathbb{V}$$ and $$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}a\sqrt{T}(D_tF_t\iota_N)_i(L_t^\ell)_i=a\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{L}^\ell,$$ which are both $O_p(\sqrt{NT})$ by equations (\ref{eq_ZgBV}) and (\ref{eq_ZgBL}) by Lemma \ref{lm_Z_gamma}.
Furthermore, we have $$\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}(B_{3t})_{ij}V_{t,j}\right](L_t^\ell)_i=\sum_{t=1}^TL_t^{\ell\prime} B_{3t}^-V_t=\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{B}_3^-\mathbb{V}=O_p\left(\sqrt{NT}\right)$$ by equation (\ref{eq_lbv}) in Lemma \ref{lm_LQV}.
Therefore,
\begin{align*}
&\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}E\left(\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}^2|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1}\right)\\
=&\sum_{t=1}^T4\sigma_0^4\mathrm{tr}\left(B_{3t}^{-\prime}B_{3t}^{-}\right)+\sigma_0^4\sum_{t=1}^T\mathrm{tr}\left(\sum_{h=0}^\infty B_{1t}^\ell P_{t-1,h}P_{t-1,h}'B_{1t}^{\ell\prime}\right)+\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}\sigma_0^2(\widetilde{C}_t)_i^2\\
&+\sum_{t=1}^T(\mu_4-\sigma_0^4)\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}^2+2\mu_3\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}(\widetilde{C}_t)_i+O_p(\sqrt{NT}).
\end{align*}
Since $B_{3t}$ is symmetric, which implies that $\mathrm{tr}(B_{3t}^2)=2tr(B_{3t}^{-\prime}B_{3t}^-)+\sum_{i=1}^{N_t}(B_{3t})_{ii}^2$. So
$$\frac{1}{NT}E\left(\mathfrak{q}_{n,ti}^2|\mathcal{F}_{n,t,i-1}\right)=\frac{1}{NT}\sigma^2_{\mathcal{Q}_n}+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),$$
which proves condition (ii).
\end{proof}



\subsection*{Additional lemmas for the TWFE case}
Recall that $\mathbb{L}^\ell$ and $\mathbb{R}^\ell$ are defined above. We can write $\mathbb{L}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_1^\ell \mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$ and $\mathbb{R}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_2^\ell \mathcal{Q}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$, where $\mathbb{B}_1^\ell = \mathrm{blkdiag}(B_{11}^\ell,\ldots,B_{1T}^\ell)$, $\mathbb{B}_2^\ell = \mathrm{blkdiag}(B_{21}^\ell,\ldots,B_{2T}^\ell)$ are $n\times(\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}N_t)$ matrices,
$\mathbb{V}_{\infty}\equiv
(V_T',V_{T-1}',\ldots,V_1'$, $V_0',V_{-1}',\ldots)'$ is infinity dimensional vector,
$$\mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell = \begin{pmatrix}
\mathbf{0}_{N_0 \times N_T} & \mathbf{0}_{N_0 \times N_{T-1}} & \dots & \mathbf{0}_{N_0 \times N_1} & B_{10}P_0^{(0)} & B_{10}P_0^{(1)} &  \dots \\
\mathbf{0}_{N_1 \times N_T} & \mathbf{0}_{N_1 \times N_{T-1}} & \dots & B_{11}P_1^{(0)} & B_{11}P_1^{(1)} & B_{11}P_1^{(2)} &  \dots \\
\vdots & \vdots & \iddots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots &  \ddots \\
\mathbf{0}_{N_{T-1} \times N_T} & B_{1,T-1}P_{T-1}^{(0)} & \dots & B_{1,T-1}P_{T-1}^{(T-2)} & B_{1,T-1}P_{T-1}^{(T-1)} & B_{1,T-1}P_{T-1}^{(T)} &  \dots
\end{pmatrix},$$ is $(\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}N_t)\times\infty$ matrix, and $\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}^\ell$ is defined analogously.
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:Pinfty-UB}
Under Assumption~\ref{asp_A2}, both $\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell$ and $\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}^\ell$ are UB. Consequently,
$\mathbb{B}_1^\ell \mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell$ and $\mathbb{B}_2^\ell \mathcal{Q}_\infty^\ell$ are also UB.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We only prove the claim for $\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell$, since the proof for $\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}^\ell$ is identical.
By Assumption~\ref{asp_A2} and Lemma \ref{lm33},
\[
\sup_{N,T}\|\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell\|_{\infty}
\leq
\sup_{N,T}\sup_{0\le t\le T-1}
\left\|
\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}\mathrm{abs}\!\left(B_{1t}P_t^{(h)}D_{t-h}\right)
\right\|_{\infty}
<\infty,
\]
and
\[
\sup_{N,T}\|\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell\|_{1}
\leq
\sup_{N,T}\sup_{t\le T-1}
\left\|
\sum_{h=t}^{\infty}\mathrm{abs}\!\left(D_h'B_{1h}P_h^{(h-t)}\right)
\right\|_{1}
<\infty.
\]
Hence $\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell$ is UB. Since $\mathbb{B}_1^\ell$ is UB by Assumption~\ref{asp_A2},
$\mathbb{B}_1^\ell\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^\ell$ is also UB. The proof for
$\mathbb{B}_2^\ell\mathcal{Q}_{\infty}^\ell$ is the same.
\end{proof}
\begin{lemma}\label{lem:infinite-qf-homo}
Suppose Assumption~2 holds. Let $\Pi_n$ be an $n\times n$ nonstochastic matrix that is UB, and let $\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}$ and $\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}$ be two nonstochastic $n\times\infty$ matrices that are UB. Define $\mathbb{M}_n\equiv \mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}$. Then
\begin{align}
E\!\left[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\Pi_n
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\right]
=
\sigma_0^2\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n),\label{eq_Einfty}    
\end{align}
and
\[
\mathrm{Var}\!\left[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\Pi_n
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\right]
=
(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}^2
+
\sigma_0^4\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')
+
\sigma_0^4\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n^2).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We have
\[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\Pi_n
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
=
\mathbb{V}_{\infty}'\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{V}_{\infty}
=
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_j
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_k .
\]

Since $\Pi_n$, $\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}$ and $\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}$ are UB, the $n\times n$ matrix $\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'$ is UB, and hence
$\mathrm{tr}\!\left(
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\right)=O(n)$.
Similarly, the $n\times n$ matrices
$\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}'\Pi_n'\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'$
and
$\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'$
are also UB, and thus their traces are $O(n)$.
Therefore, we have
\[
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
\bigl|(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}\bigr|^2
=
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')
=
\mathrm{tr}\!\left(
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}'\Pi_n'
\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\right)<\infty.
\]
where the cyclic permutation of factors is legitimate because the matrix $\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}'\Pi_n'\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
$ is
$n\times n$ and hence finite-dimensional.
Additionally,
$\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
\bigl|(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}\bigr|^2<\infty,$ and
\[
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
\bigl|(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}(\mathbb{M}_n)_{kj}\bigr|
\le
\frac12
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
\left[
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}^2+(\mathbb{M}_n)_{kj}^2
\right]
=
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}^2
=
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')
<\infty.
\]

Hence the relevant series are absolutely summable. In particular, Tonelli's theorem is used in the
following two steps. First, for the expectation,
\[
E\!\left[
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_j
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_k
\right]
=
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}
E\!\left[
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_j
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_k
\right].
\]
Since the elements of $\mathbb{V}_{\infty}$ are i.i.d. with mean zero and variance $\sigma_0^2$,
only the terms with $j=k$ survive, so
\[
E\!\left[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\Pi_n
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\right]
=
\sigma_0^2
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}
=
\sigma_0^2\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n).
\]

Second, for the second moment, Tonelli's theorem justifies the expansion
\[
E\!\left[
\left(
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_j
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_k
\right)^2
\right]
=
\sum_{j,k,l,m\ge1}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{l m}
E\!\left[
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_j
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_k
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_l
(\mathbb{V}_{\infty})_m
\right].
\]
The same case-by-case classification as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lm_quadratic form} applies. The only nonzero
contributions are
\[
j=k=l=m,\qquad
j=k\neq l=m,\qquad
j=l\neq k=m,\qquad
j=m\neq k=l.
\]
Therefore,
\[
\mathrm{Var}\!\left[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\Pi_n
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\right]
=
(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}^2
+
\sigma_0^4
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}^2
+
\sigma_0^4
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}(\mathbb{M}_n)_{kj}.
\]
Finally,
\[
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}^2
=
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n'),
\qquad
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}
(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jk}(\mathbb{M}_n)_{kj}
=
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n^2),
\]
which gives the desired result.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}\label{lem:twfe-master-qf}
Suppose Assumptions~1$'$, 2, 7 and \ref{asp_A2} hold. Let \(\Pi_n\) be either \(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\) or
\(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\), and let \(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\) and \(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\) be two nonstochastic
\(n\times\infty\) matrices that are UB. Then
\[
E\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]=
O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr).
\]
Moreover,
\[
\mathrm{Var}\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]
=O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr).
\]
Consequently,
\[
\frac{1}{NT}
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\Bigl[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\Bigr]
=
O_p\!\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\]
\[
\frac{1}{NT}
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
-\frac{1}{NT}
E\Bigl[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\Bigr]
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{T\sqrt{N}}+\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right),
\]
where
\(
\frac{1}{NT}
E\Bigl[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\Bigr]
=
O(1)
\)
and
\(
\frac{1}{NT}
E\Bigl[
(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})
\Bigr]
=
O\!\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\!\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\)
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Lemma~B.3 of Meng and Yang (2026b), both $\widebar{\mathbb{P}}$ and $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}$ are UB.
Hence \(\Pi_n\) is UB in either case.
The expectation formula follows directly from Lemma~\ref{lem:infinite-qf-homo}:
\[
E\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]
=
\sigma_0^2\,\mathrm{tr}\Bigl(\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\Bigr).
\]
Moreover,
\begin{align}
\bigl|\mathrm{tr}\bigl(\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\bigr)\bigr|
&\le
\mathrm{rank}\bigl(\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\bigr)\,
\bigl\|\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\bigr\|_2
\nonumber\\
&\le
\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\,
\Bigl\|
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\Bigr\|_1^{1/2}
\Bigl\|
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\Bigr\|_\infty^{1/2}
=
O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr),\label{eq_rank}
\end{align}
Therefore,
\[
E\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]
=
O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr).
\]

For the variance, let
$\mathbb{M}_n\equiv \mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}.$
By Lemma~\ref{lem:infinite-qf-homo},
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{Var}\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]
&=
(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}^2
+\sigma_0^4\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')
+\sigma_0^4\,\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n^2).
\end{align*}
As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:infinite-qf-homo},
$$\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')
=
\mathrm{tr}\!\left(
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}'\Pi_n'
\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\right),\qquad\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n^2)
=
\mathrm{tr}\!\left(
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\Pi_n\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}'
\right).$$ Moreover, $\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(\mathbb{M}_n)_{jj}^2
\le
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n').$
Hence, by the same argument as for the equation (\ref{eq_rank}),
\[
\bigl|\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n\mathbb{M}_n')\bigr|
=
O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr),
\qquad
\bigl|\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{M}_n^2)\bigr|
=
O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr).
\]
Therefore,
\[
\mathrm{Var}\Bigl[(\mathbb{A}_{1,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})'\Pi_n(\mathbb{A}_{2,\infty}\mathbb{V}_{\infty})\Bigr]
=O\bigl(\mathrm{rank}(\Pi_n)\bigr).
\]
Since $\widebar{\mathbb{P}}$ is the orthogonal projection matrix onto the column space of the
two-way fixed effects design matrix $(\mathbb{D},\mathbb{J})$, we have
\(
\mathrm{rank}(\widebar{\mathbb{P}})
=
N+T-1,
\)
where the subtraction of one degree of freedom is due to the normalization on the time fixed effects. Moreover,
\(
\mathrm{rank}(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}})=n-N-T+1=O(NT),
\)
so the stated expectation and stochastic orders follow. The stochastic orders follow from Chebyshev's inequality.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}\label{lem:twfe-LV-lln}
Under the Assumptions~1$'$, 2, 7 and \ref{asp_A2}, the following results hold:
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-1} \\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{T\sqrt{N}}+\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-2} \\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-3} \\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{T\sqrt{N}}+\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right). \label{eq:twfe-LV-4}\\
\frac{1}{NT}
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
-
\frac{1}{NT}
E
\left(
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
\right)
&=
O_p\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-5} \\
\frac{1}{NT}
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
-
\frac{1}{NT}
E
\left(
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
\right)
&=
O_p\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right). \label{eq:twfe-LV-6}
\end{align}

The expectations satisfy:
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\bigr)&=O(1), &
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\bigr)&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-7} \\
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right). \label{eq:twfe-LV-8b}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}
E
\left(
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
\right)
=O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),\qquad\frac{1}{NT}
E
\left(
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
\right)
=O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right). \label{eq:twfe-LV-10}
\end{align}
Specifically,
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr) &= \frac{\sigma_0^2}{NT}\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'B_{t+1}^\ell P_{t}^{(t+h-T)}B_{2,T-h}'D_{T-h}\right] \notag\\
&\quad + \frac{\sigma_0^2}{NT}\mathrm{tr}\left( \mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q} \mathcal{B}_{2\infty} \mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{\ell\prime} \mathbb{B}_1^{\ell\prime}\right) = O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right), \label{eq:twfe-LV-8a}
\end{align}
where $B_{t+1}^\ell \equiv B_{1,t+1}^\ell B_{1,t}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
%By definition, we can express $\mathbb{L}^\ell$ and $\mathbb{R}^\ell$ as $\mathbb{L}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_1^\ell \mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$ and $\mathbb{R}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_2^\ell \mathcal{Q}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$, where $\mathbb{B}_1^\ell = \mathrm{blkdiag}(B_{11}^\ell,\ldots,B_{1T}^\ell)$ and $\mathbb{B}_2^\ell = \mathrm{blkdiag}(B_{21}^\ell,\ldots,B_{2T}^\ell)$. Matrix $\mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell$ is defined as
We have $\mathbb{L}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_1^\ell \mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$ and $\mathbb{R}^\ell = \mathbb{B}_2^\ell \mathcal{Q}_\infty^\ell \mathbb{V}_\infty$.
Moreover, we can write $\mathbb{B}_2 \mathbb{V} = \mathcal{B}_{2\infty}\mathbb{V}_\infty$, where $\mathcal{B}_{2\infty}$ is an $n \times \infty$ matrix defined as
$$\mathcal{B}_{2\infty} = \begin{pmatrix}
\mathbf{0}_{N_1 \times N_T} & \dots & \mathbf{0}_{N_1 \times N_2} & B_{21} & \mathbf{0}_{N_1 \times N_0} & \dots \\
\mathbf{0}_{N_2 \times N_T} & \dots & B_{22} & \mathbf{0}_{N_2 \times N_1} & \mathbf{0}_{N_2 \times N_0} & \dots \\
\vdots & \iddots & \vdots & \vdots & \vdots & \ddots \\
B_{2T} & \dots & \mathbf{0}_{N_T \times N_2} & \mathbf{0}_{N_T \times N_1} & \mathbf{0}_{N_T \times N_0} & \dots
\end{pmatrix}.$$
Since the matrices $\mathbb{B}_1^\ell \mathcal{P}_\infty^\ell$, $\mathbb{B}_2^\ell \mathcal{Q}_\infty^\ell$, and $\mathcal{B}_{2\infty}$ are all UB according to Assumption~\ref{asp_A2} and Lemma \ref{lem:Pinfty-UB}, Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-master-qf} can be directly applied to establish \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-1}--\eqref{eq:twfe-LV-4}, and \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-7}--\eqref{eq:twfe-LV-8b}.

To derive the explicit expression \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-8a}, we notice that $\widebar{\mathbb{P}}$ is the projection matrix onto the column space of $[\mathbb{D}, \mathbb{J}]$. We can naturally decompose this into the projection onto $\mathbb{D}$ plus the projection onto the part of $\mathbb{J}$ that is orthogonal to $\mathbb{D}$. Therefore, we have
\(
\widebar{\mathbb{P}} = \mathbb{P} + \mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}.
\)
Then the expectation splits into two terms:
\[
E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr) = E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{P}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr) + E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr).
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lm_LQV}, the first term yields the double-sum trace formula exactly as in \eqref{eq_ELPV}. For the second term, applying equation \eqref{eq_Einfty}, we have:
\begin{align*}
E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&= E\bigl( \mathbb{V}_{\infty}^{\prime} \mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{\ell\prime} \mathbb{B}_1^{\ell\prime} \mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q} \mathcal{B}_{2\infty} \mathbb{V}_{\infty} \bigr)\\
&= \sigma_0^2 \, \mathrm{tr}\bigl( \mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q} \mathcal{B}_{2\infty} \mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{\ell\prime} \mathbb{B}_1^{\ell\prime} \bigr).
\end{align*}
Combining these two parts directly yields \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-8a}.


The same argument also applies to the residualized time-effect projection
\(
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\). Since
\(\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q})=T-1\), Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-master-qf} gives \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-5}--\eqref{eq:twfe-LV-6}, and their expectations satisfy \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-10}.
\end{proof}



\begin{lemma}\label{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}
Under Assumptions~1$'$, 2, 3, 4$'$, 5--7 and \ref{asp_A2}, we have
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-1}
\\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-2}
\\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-3}
\\
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}-\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
&=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-4}
\end{align}
Moreover,
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\qquad
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-5}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
&=O(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
=O(1).
\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-6}
\end{align}
The results in equations \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-1}--\eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-4} remain valid when \(\mathbb{Z}\) is replaced by
\(\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}\), where \(\widetilde{\Gamma}=\mathrm{blkdiag}(I_2,\sqrt{T},I_k)\).
Furthermore,
\begin{align}
\sqrt{T}\,\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O_p(N\sqrt{T}),\qquad
T\,\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O(NT).\label{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-7}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write
\(
\mathbb{Z}=(\mathbb{Z}_1,\mathbb{Z}_2,\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)},\mathbb{Z}_4),
\)
where \(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\) is the listing regressor and \(\mathbb{Z}_4\) collects the remaining \(k\) columns.
As in the one-way case, \(\mathbb{B}_1\mathbb{Z}_1,\mathbb{B}_1\mathbb{Z}_2,
\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_1\), and \(\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}_2\) are all of the form
\(\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{L}^{\ell}\) or \(\mathbb{C}+\mathbb{R}^{\ell}\), while
\(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\) and the columns of \(\mathbb{Z}_4\) are of the form \(\mathbb{C}\).% Here\(\mathbb{C}\) denotes a generic deterministic vector with uniformly bounded elements.

We first consider the \(\mathbb{Z}\)-\(\mathbb{V}\) terms. Let
\(\Pi_n\in\{\widebar{\mathbb{Q}},\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\}\). Every element of
\(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\) can be reduced to terms of the form
\[
\mathbb{C}'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V},
\qquad
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}.
\]
Since Lemma~B.3 of Meng and Yang (2026) implies that
\(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\) and \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\) are UB, elements in \(\mathbb{B}_2'\Pi_n\mathbb{C}\)
are still uniformly bounded. Therefore, by equation~\eqref{eq_cv} in
Lemma~\ref{lm_CQL},
\[
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{C}'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\]
For the second component, Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-LV-lln} gives
\[
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\]
with
\[
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}\bigr)
=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\]
Combining these bounds proves equations \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-1}, \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-2}, and
\eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-5}.

Next consider the \(\mathbb{Z}\)-\(\mathbb{Z}\) terms. Expanding block by block, every element of
\(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{B}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}\) is a sum of terms of the following types:
\[
\mathbb{C}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{C}_2,
\qquad
\mathbb{C}'\Pi_n\mathbb{L}^{\ell},
\qquad
\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{R}^{\ell}.
\]
The first type is deterministic, and
\(
\mathbb{C}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{C}_2=O(NT),
\)
because \(\Pi_n\) is UB and \(\mathbb{C}_1,\mathbb{C}_2\) have uniformly bounded elements.

For the second type, \(\Pi_n\mathbb{C}\) is deterministic with uniformly bounded elements. 
Applying equation (\ref{eq_cl}), together with Lemma~\ref{lm_lag}, gives
\[
\frac{1}{NT}\mathbb{C}'\Pi_n\mathbb{L}^{\ell}
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\]
The expectation is zero. 

The third type is controlled by equations
\eqref{eq:twfe-LV-1}--\eqref{eq:twfe-LV-2} and the expectation bounds in \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-7}. Therefore, equations \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-3} and \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-4} hold, and their expectations satisfy equation
\eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-6}.

It remains to verify the statements involving \(\widetilde{\Gamma}\). The matrix \(\widetilde{\Gamma}\)
only multiplies the listing column by \(\sqrt{T}\). Since
\(\Pi_n\in\{\widebar{\mathbb{Q}},\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\}\) is still UB, the same sparse-vector calculations as in
Lemma~\ref{lm_Lambda} give
\[
\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=O(1),
\qquad
\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{C}'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),
\]
and
\[
\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{L}^{\ell\prime}\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),
\qquad
\frac{\sqrt{T}}{NT}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\Pi_n\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{V}
=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\]
Together with the bounds already established for the non-listing columns, these equations imply that 
\eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-1}--\eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-4} remain valid after replacing \(\mathbb{Z}\) by
\(\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}\). They also imply
\[
\sqrt{T}\,\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
=O_p\left(N\sqrt{T}\right),
\qquad
T\,\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)'}\mathbb{B}_1'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{B}_2\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
=O(NT),
\]
which is equation \eqref{eq:twfe-ZV-ZZ-7}.
\end{proof}




\begin{lemma}\label{lem:twfe-bridge}
Under Assumptions~1$'$, 2, 3, 4$'$, 5--7 and \ref{asp_A2}, we have
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)
&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-bridge-1}
\\
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\bigr)
&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-bridge-2}
\\
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{G}_0'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\bigr)
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\bigr)
&=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\label{eq:twfe-bridge-3}
\end{align}
where
\(
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)=O(1),\) \(
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\bigr)=O(1),\) and
\(
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\bigr)=O(1).
\)
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Recall that
\(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}=\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\), \(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}=\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}+\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ},
\)
and similarly
\(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}=\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\), \(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}=\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}+\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}.
\)
Therefore,
\[
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)
-
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)
=
\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}^{\circ\prime}\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr),
\]
where \(\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\) has the same structure as in the one-way case, namely
\(
\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}=\bigl(\mathbb{L}^{\ell},\mathbb{R}^{\ell},0_{n\times (k+1)}\bigr),
\)
up to relabeling of the coefficient matrices. Hence, Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-LV-lln} implies that the right-hand
side is of order \(O(\frac{1}{T})+O(\frac{1}{N})\), which proves \eqref{eq:twfe-bridge-1}. Since $\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)$ is $O(1)$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}, therefore $\frac{1}{NT}E\bigl(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}\bigr)=O(1)$.

Equations \eqref{eq:twfe-bridge-2} and \eqref{eq:twfe-bridge-3} are proved in the same way after replacing
\(\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\) by \(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\). Under Assumptions~3 and 5, \(\mathbb{G}_0=\mathbb{W}\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}\)
is UB, so Lemmas~\ref{lem:twfe-LV-lln} and
\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ} continue to apply.
\end{proof}





\section{QMLE}
\subsection{The first and second order conditions of QMLE}\label{Foc}
For the concentrated likelihood function (7), the first order derivatives are
$$\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial\theta}=\left(\begin{aligned}
\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial \rho}\\
\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial \delta}\\
\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial \sigma^2}
\end{aligned}\right)=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{\sigma^2}\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)-\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}\right)\\
&\frac{1}{\sigma^2}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)\\
&-\frac{n}{2\sigma^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)'\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)
\end{aligned}\right),$$
where we denote $\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)=\mathbb{Q(S(\rho)Y-Z}\delta)$ for simplicity. The second order derivatives are
{
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{2}
\begin{align*}
\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}=
-\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2}\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}+\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{S(\rho)}^{-1}\mathbb{W}\right) & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{QWY} & \displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2}\mathbb{Z'QZ} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)'\mathbb{QWY} & \displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)'\mathbb{QZ} & \displaystyle-\frac{n}{2\sigma^4}+\frac{1}{\sigma^6}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)'\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)
\end{pmatrix}.    
\end{align*} 
At $\theta_0$, we have
$$\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{(WY)'QV}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\\
&\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{Z'QV}\\
&-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'QV}
\end{aligned}\right)=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V})'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z\delta}_0)'\mathbb{QV}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\\
&\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{Z'QV}\\
&-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'QV}
\end{aligned}\right),$$
and the information matrix is $\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\equiv E\left(-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\right)=\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}-\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}$, where $\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}=$
\begin{align*}
\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)\right]+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0\right) & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)\right] & \displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left(\mathbb{Z'QZ}\right) & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0\right) & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}
\end{pmatrix},
\end{align*}
$\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}=$
\begin{align*}
\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{2}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{PG}_0\mathbb{V}\right]+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{G}_0\right) & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\mathbb{Z'}\mathbb{PG}_0\mathbb{V}\right] & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}E\left(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{PG}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0\right)+\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{P}\mathbb{G}_0\right)& \displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}E\left(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{Z}\right) & \displaystyle\frac{N}{n\sigma_0^4}
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{align*}

By Lemma \ref{lm_ZPZ}, all expectation terms in $\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}$ are $O(\frac{1}{T})$.
Since $\mathbb{G}_0=\mathrm{blkdiag}(W_1S_1^{-1},\cdots,W_TS_T^{-1})$, 
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{P}\mathbb{G}_0\right)\right|&=\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{D}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\mathbb{D}'\mathbb{G}_0\right)\right|=\frac{1}{n}\left|\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathcal{T}^{-1}\mathbb{D}'\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\right)\right|\leq\frac{1}{c_TnT}\left|\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{D}'\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\right)\right|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{c_TnT}\sum_{t=1}^T\left|\mathrm{tr}\left(D_t'W_tS_t^{-1}D_t\right)\right|=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right),
\end{align*}
where the first inequality holds because $T_i>c_TT$ by Assumption 1 and the last equality holds because $D_t'W_tS_t^{-1}D_t$ is an N-dimensional UB matrix. Similarly, we can prove $\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{G}_0\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)$. Thus, $\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}=O(\frac{1}{T})$, which means that $\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}=\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}+O(\frac{1}{T})$.
We also have $\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}\Gamma+O(\frac{1}{T})$ by Lemmas \ref{lm15}-\ref{lm_Lambda}.

\subsection{The variance of the gradient}\label{B2}
Denote 
$$E\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta'}\right)\equiv\left(\begin{array}{ccc}
E_{\rho\rho}&*&*\\E_{\delta\rho}& E_{\delta\delta'}&*\\
E_{\sigma^2\rho}& E_{\sigma^2\delta'}&E_{\sigma^2\sigma^2}
\end{array}\right),$$
where
\begin{align*}
E_{\rho\rho}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right)^2\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}'\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)^2+\right.\\
&\frac{2}{\sigma_0^4}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{2}{\sigma_0^4}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}\mathbb{V}'\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0-\frac{2}{\sigma_0^2}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)+\\
&\left.\frac{2}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}'\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0-\frac{2}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)
-\frac{2}{\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)'\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)\right]+\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}^2\\%\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0
&+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{G}_0')+\frac{2\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)_i\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}+\frac{2\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)_i\mathbb{G}_{0,ii},
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
E_{\delta\rho}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right)\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^4_0}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)+\frac{\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime})_{\cdot,i}\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}+\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0\right),
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
E_{\sigma^2\rho}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\left(-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'V}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right)\right]\\
=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^4}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^4}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}+\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)+\right.\\
&\left.\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^6}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}
+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^6}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^6}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right]\\
=&\frac{\mu_4-\sigma_0^4}{2n\sigma_0^6}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)+\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}\sum_{i=1}^n(\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)_i+\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)_i,
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
E_{\delta\delta'}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^4_0}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{VV'}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)=
\frac{1}{n}E\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^4_0}\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{s=1}^T\widetilde{Z}^{*\prime}_tV_tV'_s\widetilde{Z}^{*}_s\right)\\
=&\frac{1}{n}E\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^4_0}\sum_{t=1}^T\widetilde{Z}^{*\prime}_tV_tV'_t\widetilde{Z}^{*}_t\right)=
\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right),
\end{align*}
where the third equality holds because the fact that $\widetilde{Z}^{*}_t$ is independent with $V_s$ when $t\leq s$.
\begin{align*}
E_{\sigma^2\delta'}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\left(-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'V}\right)\mathbb{V'}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right]=\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*)_{i,\cdot},
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
E_{\sigma^2\sigma^2}=&\frac{1}{n}E\left[\left(-\frac{n}{2\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'V}\right)^2\right]=\frac{\mu_4-\sigma_0^4}{4\sigma_0^8}.
\end{align*}
Therefore, 
{
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{2}
\begin{align*}
&E\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)=\\
&\begin{pmatrix}
\left\{\begin{gathered}
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)'\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)\right]\\
+\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{G}_0')
\end{gathered}\right\}& \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}E\left[\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0+\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)\right] & \displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0}\widetilde{\Gamma}E\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*}\right)\widetilde{\Gamma} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0) & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}
\end{pmatrix}+\\
&\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}^2+\frac{2\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n\left[E(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right]_i\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}& \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime})_{\cdot,i}\mathbb{G}_{0,ii} & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{2n\sigma_0^6}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)+\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}\sum_{i=1}^n\left[E(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right]_i & \displaystyle\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*\widetilde{\Gamma})_{i,\cdot} & \displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{4\sigma_0^8}
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{align*}
}
The first matrix above is equal to $\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma+O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)$ by Lemma \ref{lm_Z*}. Denote the second matrix as $\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma$, 
Since $\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*=\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}+\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ$ and $\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*=\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{Z}+\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^\circ$, where $E(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ)=E(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^\circ)=0$, it follows that
$\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*)_{i,\cdot}=\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*\widetilde{\Gamma})_{i,\cdot}=0$ and $\sum_{i=1}^n\left(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right)_i=0$ because $\iota_n'\mathbb{Q}=0$. Therefore, we have
\begin{align*}
\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}^2+\frac{2\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^n\left[E(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)+\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0\right]_i\mathbb{G}_{0,ii}& \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}\sum_{i=1}^nE(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*)_{\cdot,i}\mathbb{G}_{0,ii} & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{2n\sigma_0^6}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0) & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{4\sigma_0^8}
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{align*}
When $v_{it}$ are normally distributed, $\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=0$ because $\mu_3=0$ and $\mu_4-3\sigma^4=0$ for normal distribution. 
%Denote the third matrix above as $\Xi_{\theta_0,n}$, which is a zero matrix when the panel is balanced \textcolor{blue}{because $\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D}=0$ for balanced panel.} 
Hence, 
$$E\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)=\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma+\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma+O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right).$$

\subsection{Some properties of the Hessian matrix}\label{B.3}
For Hessian matrix $\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma$, we have the following results:
\begin{enumerate}[(1).]
\item Hessian matrix is Lipschitz continuous at $\theta_0$, which means
\begin{align}
-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)=\|\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1),\label{eq_H_conti}
\end{align}
\item Hessian matrix is consistent at $\theta_0$, which means
\begin{align}
-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),\label{eq_H_consi}
\end{align}
\item The information matrix $\Sigma_{\theta_0}\equiv\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma$ is nonsingular.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof of (1)}

Since 
\begin{align*}
&-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)=-\frac{1}{n}\times\\
&\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\right)\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}+\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathbb{G(\rho)}^2-\mathbb{G}_0^2\right] & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{QWY} & \displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}\right]'\mathbb{QWY} & \displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}\right]'\mathbb{QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma} & \left[\begin{gathered}
\displaystyle-\frac{n}{2\sigma^4_0}+\frac{1}{\sigma^6}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)'\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)\\
+\frac{n}{2\sigma^4}
-\frac{1}{\sigma^6_0}\mathbb{V'QV}
\end{gathered}\right]
\end{pmatrix}
\end{align*}
where $(\frac{1}{n\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0})\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}=\frac{1}{n\bar{\sigma}^4}\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}(\sigma_0^2-\sigma^2)$, $\bar{\sigma}^2$ lies between $\sigma^2$ and $\sigma_0^2$. Since $\mathbb{WY}=\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})$, the term $\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}$ can be decomposed as a sum of quadratic forms involving $\mathbb{Z}$ and $\mathbb{V}$, as shown in equation (\ref{eq_WYQWY}). According to Lemma \ref{lm15}, it follows that $\frac{1}{n\bar{\sigma}^4}\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}=O_p(1)$. Thus, $\|(\frac{1}{n\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0})\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}\|_2\leq \|(\sigma^2_0-\sigma^2)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$.
Similarly, we can prove $\|(\frac{1}{n\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0})\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{QWY}\|_2\leq\|(\sigma^2_0-\sigma^2)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$ and $\|(\frac{1}{n\sigma^2}-\frac{1}{n\sigma^2_0})\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\|_2\leq\|(\sigma^2_0-\sigma^2)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$ by Lemmas \ref{lm15} and \ref{lm_Lambda}.
Additionally, $\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}[\mathbb{G(\rho)}^2-\mathbb{G}_0^2]=\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{tr}[\mathbb{G(\bar{\rho})}^3](\rho-\rho_0)$, where $\bar{\rho}$ lies between $\rho$ and $\rho_0$. Note that
\begin{align}
\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{G}(\bar{\rho})\|_\infty&=\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{WS}^{-1}(\bar{\rho})\|_\infty\leq\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{W}\|_{\infty}\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{S}^{-1}(\Bar{\rho})\|_{\infty}\nonumber\\
&=\left(\sup_{N,T}\sup_t\|W_t\|_{\infty}\right)\left(\sup_{N,T}\sup_t\|S_t(\Bar{\rho})^{-1}\|_{\infty}\right)<\infty,\label{eq_Grho}
\end{align}
where the second equality holds because $\mathbb{W}$ and $\mathbb{S}^{-1}(\Bar{\rho})$ are block diagonal matrix and the last inequality holds by equation (9) and Assumption 5.
Thus, $\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{G}(\bar{\rho})^3\|_\infty\leq\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{G}(\bar{\rho})\|_\infty^3\leq\infty$, which implies that $\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{tr}[\mathbb{G(\bar{\rho})}^3]=O(1)$. 
So $\|\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}[\mathbb{G(\rho)}^2-\mathbb{G}_0^2]\|_2\leq\|\rho-\rho_0\|_2\cdot O(1)$.
Moreover,
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{n}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}\right]'\mathbb{QWY}\\
=&\frac{1}{n}\left[\frac{1}{\sigma^4}(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{WY}+\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)+\left(\frac{1}{\sigma^4}-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\right)\mathbb{V}\right]'\mathbb{QWY}\\
=&(\rho_0-\rho)\frac{1}{n\sigma^4}(\mathbb{WY})'\mathbb{QWY}+[\widetilde{\Gamma}^{-1}(\delta_0-\delta)]'\frac{1}{n\sigma^4}\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QWY}-(\sigma^2-\sigma^2_0)\frac{2}{n\bar{\sigma}^6}\mathbb{V'QWY}.
\end{align*}
Since all the coefficients of $\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)$ in the above expression are $O_p(1)$ by Lemmas \ref{lm15} and \ref{lm_Lambda}, we therefore have $\|\frac{1}{n}[\frac{1}{\sigma^4}\mathbb{\widetilde{V}}(\zeta)-\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}]'\mathbb{QWY}\|_2\leq\|\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$ by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Therefore, we can similarly prove that the remaining elements of the above matrix are $\|\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$.

%=&\|\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)

\textbf{Proof of (2)}

We have
\begin{align*}
&-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2 L_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=-\frac{1}{n}\times\\
&\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\left[\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}-E\left(\mathbb{(WY)'}\mathbb{QWY}\right)\right] & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\left[\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{QWY}-E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{QWY}\right)\right] & \displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^2_0}\left[\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}-E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)\right] & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\left[\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{QWY}-E\left(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{QWY}\right)\right] & \displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_0^4}\left[\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}-E\left(\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)\right] & \displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma^6_0}\left[\mathbb{V'QV}-E\left(\mathbb{V'QV}\right)\right]
\end{pmatrix}.
\end{align*}
Since $\mathbb{WY}=\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})$, which is a combination of $\mathbb{Z}$ and $\mathbb{V}$, it follows that elements in the matrix above are $O_p(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}})$ by Lemmas \ref{lm15} and \ref{lm_Lambda}.

\textbf{Proof of (3)}

The information matrix is nonsingular if and only if for any vector $\boldsymbol{a}$, $\Sigma_{\theta_0}\boldsymbol{a} = 0$ implies $\boldsymbol{a}=0$. Let $\boldsymbol{a}=(a_1,\boldsymbol{a}_2',a_3)'$, where $a_1$ and $a_3$ are scalars and $\boldsymbol{a}_2$ is $(k+3)$-dimensional vector. As
\begin{align*}
\Sigma_{\theta_0}=&\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}\Gamma\\
\equiv&\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\times
\begin{pmatrix}
