\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}
+\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0\right) & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta} & \displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0\right) & \displaystyle\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)} & \displaystyle\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^2}
\end{pmatrix},
\end{align*}
where the second equality holds because $\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\Gamma\left(\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}-\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}\right)\Gamma=\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}\Gamma+O(\frac{1}{T})$, as derived in \ref{Foc}. Additionally, $\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}\equiv\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}E\left[\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)\right]$, $\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}\equiv\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}E[\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)]$ and $\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}\equiv\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}E(\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z'QZ}\widetilde{\Gamma})$. 
$\Sigma_{\theta_0}\boldsymbol{a}=0$ means
$$\begin{cases}
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\left[\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}
+\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0\right)\right]a_1+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\boldsymbol{a}_2+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0\right)a_3=0\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}a_1+\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}\boldsymbol{a}_2=0\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sigma_0^2}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0\right)a_1+\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}a_3=0
\end{cases}.$$
According to the last two equations, we have $a_3=-2\sigma_0^2\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)a_1$ and $\boldsymbol{a}_2=-\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}a_1$. Substituting them into the first equation gives
\begin{align}
\left[\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}-\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}
+\sigma_0^2\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0)-\frac{2}{n}\left(\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\right)^2\right)\right]a_1=0.\label{eq_a1}
\end{align}
Note that
\begin{align}
&\left(\begin{matrix}
\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho} &\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\\
\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta} & \mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}
\end{matrix}\right)\nonumber\\
=&\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}E\left[\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z})'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z})\right]\nonumber\\
=&\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\nonumber\\
&E\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0) & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0) & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_1'\mathbb{QZ}_1 & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_2'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0) & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_2'\mathbb{QZ}_1 & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_2'\mathbb{QZ}_2 & \displaystyle* & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0) & \displaystyle\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}_1 & \displaystyle\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}_2 & \displaystyle T\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)} & \displaystyle*\\
\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0) & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{QZ}_1 & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{QZ}_2 & \displaystyle\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)} & \displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{QZ}_4\\
\end{pmatrix}\label{eq_Matrix},
\end{align}
where $\mathbb{Z}_1$, $\mathbb{Z}_2$ and $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ are the first three columns of $\mathbb{Z}$, and $\mathbb{Z}_4$ denotes the remaining $k$ columns of $\mathbb{Z}$. Then $\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}-\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}\geq0$ because $\mathbb{(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z})'\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\widetilde{\Gamma}\mathbb{Z})$ is positive semi-definite.
Moreover, note that
$$\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0)-\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)^2=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{G}_0+\mathbb{G}_0'-\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)I_n\right)\left(\mathbb{G}_0+\mathbb{G}_0'-\frac{2}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)I_n\right)'\right]\geq0.$$

Under the above conditions, we will use Assumption 9 (i) and Assumption 9 (ii) separately to show that either $\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}-\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}$ or $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0)-\frac{2}{n}(\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0))^2\right)$ is greater than 0, which then implies from equation (\ref{eq_a1}) that $a_1=0$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item (Using Assumption 9 (i)) Because $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ is sparse binary vector with at most $N$ nonzero elements, elements in $E(\mathbb{Z})$ and $\alpha_0$ are uniformly bounded, and $\mathbb{G}_0$ is UB, we have
$\sqrt{T}E[\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{Q(G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)]=O(N\sqrt{T})$, $\sqrt{T}E[\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}_1]=O(N\sqrt{T})$, $\sqrt{T}E[\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}_2]=O(N\sqrt{T})$, $\sqrt{T}\mathbb{Z}_4'\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}=O(N\sqrt{T})$ and $T\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}=O(NT)$. Moreover,
\begin{align*}
\frac{T}{n}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}&=\frac{T}{n}\sum_{t=1}^T\iota_N'F_t'D_t'D_tF_t\iota_N-\frac{T}{n}\left(\sum_{t=1}^TD_t'D_tF_t\iota_N\right)'\mathcal{T}^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^TD_t'D_tF_t\iota_N\right)\\
&=\frac{T}{n}\sum_{t=1}^T\iota_N'F_t\iota_N-\frac{T}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathcal{T}^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^TF_t\iota_N\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^TF_t\iota_N\right)'\right]\\
&\geq\frac{T(1-c_N)N}{n}-\frac{T}{n}\sum_{i=1}^N\frac{1}{T_i}\\
&\geq \frac{1-c_N}{c_u}-\frac{1}{c_uc_TT},
\end{align*}
where the second equality holds because $D_t'D_t=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1t},\cdots,d_{Nt})$ and $F_t=\mathrm{diag}(d_{1t}(1-d_{1,t-1}),\cdots,$ $d_{Nt}(1-d_{N,t-1}))$. The first inequality follows from Assumption 1, which states that the listing effect appears at least $(1-c_N)N$ times and at most $N$ times over the entire panel. Thus
$\sum_{t=1}^T \iota_N' F_t \iota_N \ge (1-c_N)N$,
and elements in vector $\sum_{t=1}^T F_t \iota_N$ is bounded above by 1. The second inequality holds by $T_i\geq c_TT$ in Assumption 1. Therefore, $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{T}{n}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}$ exists and bounded away from zero.

Then according to Assumption 9 (i), the determinant of the matrix (\ref{eq_Matrix}) is, $$\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}T\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\left|\frac{1}{n}E\left[\mathcal{H}^{(-\gamma)}\right]\right|>0.$$
Then the matrix (\ref{eq_Matrix}) is positive definite, and therefore $\mathcal{H}_{\rho\rho}-\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}'\mathcal{H}_{\delta\delta}^{-1}\mathcal{H}_{\rho\delta}>0$.
\item (Using Assumption 9 (ii)) It is trivial.
\end{enumerate}

Once we have $a_1=0$, we then have $\boldsymbol{a}_2=\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times1}$ and $a_3=0$, which means that the information matrix is nonsingular.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Asymptotic Representation of 
$\Delta_n$}\label{B.4}
Given $$\Delta_n=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}\\
&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ\prime}\mathbb{V}\\
&\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'}\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
\end{aligned}\right),$$
where 
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P})+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{P})+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),\\
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}&=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{P})+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^2}+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
\end{align*}
by equation (\ref{eq_vpv}) in Lemma \ref{lm_VQV}. Additionally, we have
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}&=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\frac{1}{N}E\left[(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}\right]+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right),\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ\prime}\mathbb{V}\right)&=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\frac{1}{N}E\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ\prime}\mathbb{V}\right)+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\\
\end{align*}
by equation (\ref{eq_lpv}) in Lemma \ref{lm_LQV}. 
%Decompose $\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ$ into $N_t$-dimensional vectors $\widetilde{GZ}_1^\circ,\cdots,\widetilde{GZ}_T^\circ$ such that $\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ=(\widetilde{GZ}_1^{\circ\prime},\cdots,\widetilde{GZ}_T^{\circ\prime})'$. Then $$
Since $\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ}=\mathbb{PZ}^{\circ}$, and $Z_t^\circ=(D_t(I_N-F_t)D_t'M_t\mathcal{V}_t,D_tD_{t-1}'\mathcal{V}_t,\mathbf{0}_{N_t\times(k+1)})$, thus
\begin{align*}
E[\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ\prime}\mathbb{V}]=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)\\
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)\\
&\mathbf{0}_{(k+1)\times1}
\end{aligned}\right)
\end{align*}
by equations (\ref{eq_ELV}) and (\ref{eq_ELPV}) in Lemma \ref{lm_LQV}. Similarly, we have 
%Since $(G_tD_t(I_N-F_t)D_t'M_t\mathcal{V}_t,G_tD_tD_{t-1}'\mathcal{V}_t,\mathbf{0}_{N_t\times(k+1)})$, we have
\begin{align*}
E\left[(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^\circ\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}\right]
=&\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}D_{T-h}\right)\\
&+\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}\nu D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}D_{T-h}\right),
\end{align*}
where $G_t\equiv W_tS_t^{-1}$. Thus, $\Delta_n=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\varphi_n(\theta_0)+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$, $\varphi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)$ and
\begin{align}
&\varphi_n(\theta)=\nonumber\\
&\left(\begin{aligned}
&\left[\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}\nu D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}(\rho)'\mathbb{P})    
\end{aligned}\right]\\
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)\\
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{h=1}^{T-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t(\rho)^{-1}A_{t}^{(t+h-T)}(\theta)D_{T-h}\right)\\
&\mathbf{0}_{(k+1)\times1}\\
&\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}
\end{aligned}\right),\label{eq_phi_n}
\end{align}
where $G_t(\rho)=W_tS_t(\rho)^{-1}$, $A_t^{(h)}(\theta)=A_t(\theta)A_{t-1}(\theta)\cdots A_{t-h+1}(\theta)$ for $h=1,2,\cdots$, $A_t^{(0)}(\theta)=I_{N_t}$, and $A_t(\theta)=D_t[\lambda_0 (I_N-F_t)D_t'M_t+\nu_0 D_{t-1}']S_{t-1}(\rho)^{-1}$.



\subsection{Score, variance, and Hessian formulas for the TWFE QMLE}\label{secB5}

This subsection provides the TWFE counterparts of the formulas in Sections \ref{Foc}--\ref{B.4}.
Recall that \(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\) is the projection matrix onto the space spanned by the individual and time effects, and \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}=I_n-\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\). For \(\zeta=(\rho,\delta')'\), denote
\[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
=
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{Y}-\mathbb{Z}\delta
\right).
\]
Then the TWFE concentrated log-likelihood function is
\[
\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)
=
-\frac{n}{2}\log(2\pi)
-\frac{n}{2}\log\sigma^2
+\log|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|
-\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta).
\]

\textbf{The first and second order conditions}

For simplicity, denote \(\mathbb{G}(\rho)=\mathbb{W}\mathbb{S}^{-1}(\rho)\). The first order derivatives are
\[
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}{\partial\theta}
=
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^2}
(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
-
\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbb{G}(\rho)\}
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^2}
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\\
\displaystyle
-\frac{n}{2\sigma^2}
+
\frac{1}{2\sigma^4}
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\end{array}
\right).
\]
The second order derivatives are
\[
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
=
-
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^2}
(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\operatorname{tr}\{\mathbb{G}^2(\rho)\}
&
*
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^2}
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^2}
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^4}
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sigma^4}
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}
&
\displaystyle
-\frac{n}{2\sigma^4}
+
\frac{1}{\sigma^6}
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\end{array}
\right).
\]

Moreover, the information matrix is
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}
\equiv
E\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\right)
=
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}
-
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)},
\)
where \(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}=\)
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\displaystyle
\left[\begin{aligned}
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E\left[
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right\}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right\}
\right]
\\
+
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0
\right)
\end{aligned}
\right]
&
*
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E\left[
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right\}
\right]
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E\left(
\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}
\right)
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathbb{G}_0\right)
&
\displaystyle
\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}
\end{array}
\right),
\]
and \(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}=\)
\[
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\displaystyle
\frac{2}{n\sigma_0^2}
E\left[
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}
\right]
+
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0
\right)
&
*
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E\left[
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}
\right]
&
\displaystyle
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)}
&
*
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}
E\left(
\mathbb{V}'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right)
+
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0
\right)
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}
E\left(
\mathbb{V}'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{Z}
\right)
&
\displaystyle
\frac{N+T-1}{n\sigma_0^4}
\end{array}
\right).
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}, all expectation terms in
\(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}\) are
\(
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\)
It remains to control the trace terms. Since \(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\) is an orthogonal
projection matrix with rank \(N+T-1\), and \(\mathbb{G}_0\), \(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0\) are UB under Assumptions~3 and 5, we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{1}{n}
\left|
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0
\right)
\right|
\leq
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{rank}\left(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
\left\|\mathbb{G}_0\right\|_2=
O\left(\frac{N+T}{NT}\right)
=
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Similarly,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\left|
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0
\right)
\right|
\leq
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{rank}\left(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
\left\|\mathbb{G}_0\right\|_2^2
=
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\]
Thus,
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(2)}
=
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\)
which means that
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}
=
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}
+
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\)
We also have
\(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{(1)}\Gamma
+
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right)
\)
by Lemma \ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}.

\textbf{TWFE score decomposition}

Let \(\widebar{\mathbb P}=I_n-\widebar{\mathbb Q}\). Define
\(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}
=\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\),
\(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
=\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}
+\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\),
\(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}
=\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}\), and
\(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
=\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}
+\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\).
At \(\theta_0\), the scaled TWFE score can be decomposed as
\[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
=
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
-
\widebar{\Delta}_n,
\]
where
\[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
=
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}'\mathbb{V}
+
\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}
\right]
-
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)
\\[1.5ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}
\\[1.5ex]
\displaystyle
-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_0^2}
+
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}
\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}
\end{array}
\right),
\]
and
\[
\widebar{\Delta}_n
=
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{V}
+
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\left(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\delta_0
\right)'\mathbb{V}
\\[1.5ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ\prime}\mathbb{V}
\\[1.5ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}
\mathbb{V}'\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{V}
\end{array}
\right).
\]
The first component is a mean-zero score component, while
\(\widebar{\Delta}_n\) collects the nonzero mean terms generated by
concentrating out the individual and time fixed effects.


\textbf{Variance of the mean-zero score}

The mean-zero score can be written as
\[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
=
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right]'
\mathbb{V}
+
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}
-
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}
\\
\displaystyle
-\frac{\sqrt{n}}{2\sigma_0^2}
+
\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}
\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{V}
\end{array}
\right).
\]
By the same calculation as in Section~B.2 and Lemma \ref{lem:twfe-bridge}, with \(\mathbb{Q}\) replaced by \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\) and \(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0\) replaced by \(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0\), we have
\begin{align}
&E
\left[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\right]\nonumber
\\
=&
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\rho\rho,n}
&
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\rho\delta,n}
&
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\rho\sigma,n}
\\
*
&
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\delta\delta,n}
&
0
\\
*
&
*
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}
\end{array}
\right)+
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\rho,n}
&
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\delta,n}
&
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\sigma,n}
\\
*
&
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)}
&
\widebar{\Omega}_{\delta\sigma,n}
\\
*
&
*
&
\displaystyle
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{4\sigma_0^8}
\end{array}
\right)+
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),\label{eq_TWFE_VAR}
\end{align}
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\rho\rho,n}
=&
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}'
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}
\right]
\\
&+
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0^2+\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{G}_0
\right),
\\
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\rho\delta,n}
=&
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
E
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}'
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
\right]
\widetilde{\Gamma},
\\
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\delta\delta,n}
=&
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}
E
\left(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*\prime}
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
\right)
\widetilde{\Gamma},
\\
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\sigma\rho,n}
=&
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0),
\\
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\rho,n}
=&
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{n\sigma_0^4}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
(\mathbb{G}_0)_{ii}^2
+
\frac{2\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
E
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}_i
\right]
(\mathbb{G}_0)_{ii},
\\
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\delta,n}
=&
\frac{\mu_3}{n\sigma_0^4}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
(\mathbb{G}_0)_{ii}
E
\left(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}
\right)_{i,\cdot},
\\
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\sigma,n}
=&
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{2n\sigma_0^6}
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)
+
\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
E
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}_i
\right],
\\
\widebar{\Omega}_{\delta\sigma,n}
=&
\frac{\mu_3}{2n\sigma_0^6}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
E
\left[
\left(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}
\right)_{i,\cdot}^{\prime}
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Since
\(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
=
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\), and
\(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}
=
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\),
where
\(
E\!\left(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\right)=0\) and
\(E\!\left(\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}\right)=0,
\)
and since \(\iota_n'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}=0\), we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
E
\left[
\left\{
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\delta_0
\right\}_i
\right]
=
\iota_n'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\iota_n'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0E(\mathbb{Z})\delta_0
=0,
\end{aligned}
\]
and
\[
\sum_{i=1}^{n}
E
\left[
\left(
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{*}\widetilde{\Gamma}
\right)_{i,\cdot}^{\prime}
\right]
=
\left\{
\iota_n'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}E(\mathbb{Z})\widetilde{\Gamma}
\right\}^{\prime}
=
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times1}.
\]
Therefore,
\[
\widebar{\Omega}_{\rho\sigma,n}
=
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{2n\sigma_0^6}
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0),
\qquad
\widebar{\Omega}_{\delta\sigma,n}
=
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times1}.
\]

If \(v_{it}\)'s are normally distributed, then \(\mu_3=0\) and \(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4=0\). Hence
\(
\Gamma\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=0.
\)




\textbf{Some properties of the Hessian matrix}

For the Hessian matrix \(
\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma,
\)
we have the following results.

(1). The Hessian matrix is Lipschitz continuous at \(\theta_0\), that is,
\begin{align}
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
-
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\right)
=
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)
\right\|_2O_p(1).
\label{B.7}
\end{align}



(2). The Hessian matrix is consistent at \(\theta_0\), that is,
\begin{align}
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
-
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\label{B.8}
\end{align}



(3). The information matrix
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\)
is nonsingular.

\begin{proof}
For (1), the proof is the same as that of the Lipschitz continuity of the Hessian matrix in
Section~B.3. The only difference is that \(\mathbb{Q}\) is replaced by
\(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\). Since \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\) is also an orthogonal projection matrix,
the TWFE linear--quadratic form bounds in Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ} give the same orders.
Therefore,
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
-
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\right)
=
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\theta-\theta_0)
\right\|_2O_p(1).
\]

For (2), at \(\theta_0\), we have
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
=
\left(
\begin{array}{ccc}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0^2)
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{\Gamma}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}
(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{V}
\\
*
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}
\widetilde{\Gamma}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^4}
\widetilde{\Gamma}
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{V}
\\
*
&
*
&
\displaystyle
-\frac{1}{2\sigma_0^4}
+
\frac{1}{n\sigma_0^6}
\mathbb{V}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{V}
\end{array}
\right).
\]
Moreover,
\(
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
=
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
+
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}.
\)
Hence, after substituting this expression into the above Hessian matrix, its stochastic blocks are finite linear combinations of TWFE linear--quadratic forms involving \(\mathbb{Z}\) and \(\mathbb{V}\), together with nonstochastic terms. By Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}, we have
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
-
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).
\]
This proves (2).


For (3), the proof is the same as the proof of nonsingularity in Section~B.3. Specifically, after
replacing the OWFE information matrix by
\(\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\), the Schur complement argument gives the
same two alternatives as in Assumption~9$'$. Thus, under Assumption~9$'\),
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}\)
is nonsingular. This completes the proof of (1)--(3).
\end{proof}


\textbf{TWFE bias representation}

Using the decomposition of \(\widebar{\Delta}_n\) above and the identity
\(
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
=
\mathbb{P}
+
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\),
we have
\[
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}
=
\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ}
+
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{Z}^{\circ},
\]
and
\[
\widebar{\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}}^{\circ}
=
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ}
+
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}^{\circ}.
\]
Therefore, \(\widebar{\Delta}_n\) can be decomposed into the OWFE component
generated by \(\mathbb{P}\) and the time-effect component generated by
\(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\).
The leading deterministic part of the OWFE component is summarized by
\(\varphi_n(\theta)\), whose explicit expression is given in
equation~(\ref{eq_phi_n}).

%The OWFE component is exactly \(\varphi_n(\theta)\), whose explicit expression is given in equation~(\ref{eq_phi_n}).

For the time-effect component, let \(K_t\) be the \(N_t\times(T-1)\) matrix such that
\((K_1', K_2',\cdots,K_T')'=\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J}\).
Then according to equations (\ref{eq:twfe-LV-5}) and (\ref{eq:twfe-LV-6}), the additional time-effect bias component is
\begin{align}
\psi_n(\theta)
\equiv
\left(
\begin{array}{c}
\displaystyle
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{T}
\sum_{t=1}^{T}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
K_t'
G_t(\rho)
K_t
\right]
+
\frac{1}{T}
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}
\sum_{h=T-t}^{T-1}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
K_{t+1}'
\mathcal{M}_{\rho,t,h}(\theta)
K_{T-h}
\right]    
\end{aligned}
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{T}
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}
\sum_{h=T-t}^{T-1}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
K_{t+1}'
\mathcal{M}_{\lambda,t,h}(\theta)
K_{T-h}
\right]
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{T}
\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}
\sum_{h=T-t}^{T-1}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
(\mathbb{J}'\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{J})^{-1}
K_{t+1}'
\mathcal{M}_{\nu,t,h}(\theta)
K_{T-h}
\right]
\\
\displaystyle
\mathbf{0}_{k+1}
\\
\displaystyle
\frac{T-1}{2T\sigma^2}
\end{array}
\right),
\label{B.9}
\end{align}
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal{M}_{\lambda,t,h}(\theta)
=&
D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t^{-1}(\rho)
A_t^{(t+h-T)}(\theta),
\\
\mathcal{M}_{\nu,t,h}(\theta)
=&
D_{t+1}D_t'S_t^{-1}(\rho)
A_t^{(t+h-T)}(\theta),
\\
\mathcal{M}_{\rho,t,h}(\theta)
=&
G_{t+1}(\rho)
\left[
\lambda\mathcal{M}_{\lambda,t,h}(\theta)
+
\nu\mathcal{M}_{\nu,t,h}(\theta)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
We have \(\psi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)\) by equation (\ref{eq:twfe-LV-10}).
Thus, by \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-4} and \eqref{eq:twfe-LV-8a},
\begin{align}
\widebar{\Delta}_n
=
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}
\varphi_n(\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{c_uN}}
\psi_n(\theta_0)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),
\label{B.10}
\end{align}
where \(c_u=n/(NT)\), \(\varphi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)\), and
\(\psi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)\).

Furthermore, by the nonsingularity of
\(\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\) established above and the same argument as in
Section~\ref{B.4},
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)=O_p(1),\) and
\(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)=O_p(1).
\)




\section{Proof of Theorems}

\subsection{Proof of Theorem 1}
Since only $O(N)$ observations contain information about $\gamma$, this parameter exhibits a slower convergence rate compared to others. 
Denote $\theta^{(-\gamma)}$ as the vector obtained by removing $\gamma$ from $\theta$, with $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$ and $\theta^{(-\gamma)}_0$ defined analogously. We will first prove that $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\theta^{(-\gamma)}_0$, then the consistency of $\hat{\gamma}$ follows directly from its explicit expression in terms of $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$.

Recall that
$$Q_n(\theta)= E[L_n^c(\theta)]=-\frac{n}{2}\ln2\pi-\frac{n}{2}\ln\sigma^2+\ln\left|\mathbb{S}(\rho)\right|-\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}E\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}'(\zeta)\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right].$$
The consistency of $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$ comes from uniform convergence and identifiable uniqueness, we first show the uniform convergence of $\frac{1}{n}(L_n^c(\theta)-Q_n(\theta))$, and then show the identifiable uniqueness by proving the equicontinuity of $Q_n(\theta)$ and the global identified condition. Our proof follows the idea in Yu et al. (2008).

\textbf{Proof of uniform convergence}

The uniform convergence is specifically defined as $\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}|\frac{1}{n}\left(L_n^c(\theta)-Q_n(\theta)\right)|\overset{p}{\to}0.$
Denote the true value of each parameter as $\theta_0=(\rho_0,\delta_0',\sigma_0^2)$, $\zeta_0=(\rho_0,\delta_0')$. As

%Denote $\mathbb{S}_0\equiv\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)$ and $\mathbb{G}_0\equiv\mathbb{WS}^{-1}_0$.%$\mathbb{G}(\rho)=\mathbb{WS}^{-1}(\rho)$,
$$\frac{1}{n}\left(L_n^c(\theta)-Q_n(\theta)\right)=\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}\left(\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)-\frac{1}{n}E\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right),$$
we will first show $\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}\left|\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)-E\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right]\right|\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0$.
Since $\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)=\mathbb{Q(Y-\rho WY-Z\delta})$, substituting $\mathbb{Y}$ for $\mathbb{Y}=\rho_0\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}+\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V}$, we have $\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)=\mathbb{Q[V-(}\rho-\rho_0)\mathbb{WY-Z}(\delta-\delta_0)]$. Therefore
\begin{align}
\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)=&\mathbb{V'QV}+(\rho-\rho_0)^2\mathbb{(WY)'QWY}+(\delta-\delta_0)'\mathbb{Z'QZ}(\delta-\delta_0)-\nonumber\\
&2(\rho-\rho_0)\mathbb{V'QWY}-2\mathbb{V'QZ}(\delta-\delta_0)+2(\rho-\rho_0)\mathbb{(WY)'QZ}(\delta-\delta_0).\label{eq_V'V}
\end{align}
Since $\mathbb{WY}=\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})$, $\mathbb{V'QWY}=\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}$, $\mathbb{(WY)'QZ}=(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QZ}+(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QZ}+(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V)'QZ}$, and
\begin{align}
\mathbb{(WY)'QWY}=&(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V})'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}+\nonumber\\
&2(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+2(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}+2(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}.\label{eq_WYQWY}
\end{align}
Since $\mathbb{G}_0=\mathbb{WS}^{-1}_0=\mathrm{blkdiag}(W_1S_1^{-1},W_2S_2^{-1},\cdots,W_TS_T^{-1})$, where $W_tS_t^{-1}$ is UB uniformly for $t$ and $\|\sup_t \mathrm{abs}(W_tS_t^{-1})\|_\infty<\infty$ is ensured by Assumption 5 and Lemma \ref{lm33}, it follows from Lemma \ref{lm15} that
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V'QV}-\frac{1}{n}E[\mathbb{V'QV}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{n}E[\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,\\
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V})'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V})'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z'QZ}-\frac{1}{n}E[\mathbb{Z'QZ}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,\\
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QZ}-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QZ}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,\\
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V'QZ}-\frac{1}{n}E[\mathbb{V'QZ}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0-\frac{1}{n}E[\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,\\
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V)'QZ}-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{V)'QZ}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0.
\end{align*}
Additionally, according to Lemma \ref{lm_CQL}, 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V'Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{V}\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
\\
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QZ}-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)'\mathbb{QZ}]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0,
&\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0-\frac{1}{n}E[(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0)'\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0]\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0.
\end{align*}
Therefore, all polynomial coefficients in $\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)$ will converge to their expected values. 
Since $\rho$ and $\delta$ are bounded in $\Theta$, it follows that $\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}|\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)-E[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)]|\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0$. Since $\sigma^2$ is bounded away from zero, we therefore have $\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\frac{1}{n}|L_n^c(\theta)-Q_n(\theta)|\stackrel{p}{\rightarrow}0$.

\textbf{Proof of equicontinuity}

Next we prove that $\frac{1}{n}Q_n(\theta)$ is uniformly equicontinuous for $\theta\in\Theta$, which is specifically defined as for any $\epsilon>0$, there exists $\eta>0$ and $n_0\in\mathbb{N}$, such that whenever $d(\theta,\bar{\theta})<\eta$ and $n>n_0$, we have 
$$\left|\frac{1}{n}\left(Q_n(\theta)-Q_n(\bar{\theta})\right)\right|<\epsilon.$$
Since 
$$\frac{1}{n}Q_n(\theta)=-\frac{1}{2}\ln2\pi-\frac{1}{2}\ln\sigma^2+\frac{1}{n}\ln|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|-\frac{1}{2n\sigma^2}E\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}'(\zeta)\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right],$$
where $E[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}'(\zeta)\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)]=\mathbb{(\mathbb{S(\rho)}Y-Z\delta)'Q(\mathbb{S(\rho)}Y-Z\delta})$, substitute $\mathbb{Y}$ for $\mathbb{Y=S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})$, then
\begin{align}
E[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)]=&E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)'\mathbb{Q}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V})-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)\right]\nonumber\\
=&E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)'\mathbb{Q}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)\right]\nonumber\\
&+E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right]
+2E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right],\label{eq_EVTV}
\end{align}
where $E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right]=\sigma^2_0\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right]$,
and $$E\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)-\mathbb{Z\delta}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}\right]=O(N)$$
by Lemma \ref{lm15}.

Denote $\sigma^2_n\equiv\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\mathrm{tr}[(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1})'\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}]$. Since
\begin{align*}
\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{PS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right]&=\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathbb{D}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\mathbb{D'S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\right]\\
&\leq\frac{\sigma^2_0}{c_TnT}\mathrm{tr}\left[\mathrm{abs}\left(\mathbb{D'S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{D}\right)\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right),
\end{align*}
where the inequality holds because $T_i>c_TT$ by Assumption 1 and the matrix inside the trace operation is UB. Thus, $\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{QS(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right]=\sigma^2_n+O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)$. Further substitute $\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}$ for $\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}=I_n+(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0$, then we have
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}E[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)]=&\frac{1}{n}E\left\{\left[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)\right]'\mathbb{Q}\left[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)\right]\right\}\nonumber\\
&+\sigma^2_n+o(1),\label{EVV}
\end{align}
where the first term is equal to $(\rho-\rho_0,\delta'-\delta_0')E[\mathcal{H}](\rho-\rho_0,\delta'-\delta_0')'$, and $\mathcal{H}\equiv\frac{1}{n}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\mathbb{Z})'\mathbb{Q}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\mathbb{Z})$.
Therefore, the uniform equicontinuity of $\frac{1}{n}Q_n(\theta)$ is sufficient to show the following is uniformly equicontinuous: (i) $-\frac{1}{2}\ln\sigma^2$; (ii) $\frac{1}{n}\ln|\mathbb{S(\rho)}|$;
(iii) $(\rho-\rho_0,\delta'-\delta_0')E[\mathcal{H}](\rho-\rho_0,\delta'-\delta_0')'$; (iv) $\sigma^2_n$.

Since $\sigma^2$ is bounded away from zero, (i) is obviously uniformly equicontinuous.
For (ii), $\frac{1}{n}(\ln|\mathbb{S(\rho}_1)|-\ln|\mathbb{S(\rho}_2)|)=(\rho_1-\rho_2)\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{WS}^{-1}(\Bar{\rho}))$, where $\Bar{\rho}$ lies between $\rho_1$ and $\rho_2$. According to equation (\ref{eq_Grho}), 
$\sup_{N,T}\|\mathbb{W}\mathbb{S}^{-1}(\Bar{\rho})\|_{\infty}<\infty$, so $\frac{1}{n}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{WS}^{-1}(\Bar{\rho}))$ is bounded, which means that (ii) is uniformly equicontinuous.
(iii) is uniformly equicontinuous because the parameter space $(\rho,\delta)$ is bounded and $E[\mathcal{H}]$ is $O(1)$. 
For (iv), 
\begin{align*}
&\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\{\mathrm{tr}[(\mathbb{S(\rho}_1)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1})'\mathbb{QS(\rho}_1)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}]-\mathrm{tr}[(\mathbb{S(\rho}_2)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1})'\mathbb{QS(\rho}_2)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}]\}\\
=&\frac{\sigma^2_0}{n}\{(\rho_0-\rho_1)^2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{QG}_0)+2(\rho_0-\rho_1)\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Q})-(\rho_0-\rho_2)^2\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{QG}_0)-2(\rho_0-\rho_2)\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Q})\}\\
=&2(\rho_2-\rho_1)\sigma^2_0\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Q})}{n}+(2\rho_0-\rho_1-\rho_2)(\rho_2-\rho_1)\sigma^2_0\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{QG}_0)}{n}.
\end{align*}
Since $\mathbb{G}_0$ and $\mathbb{Q}$ are UB, similarly (iv) is uniformly equicontinuous.

\textbf{Proof of global identification condition}

Then we prove that $\theta_0$, except $\gamma_0$, is identified, which is specifically defined as $\forall\theta^{(-\gamma)}\neq\theta^{(-\gamma)}_0$,
$$\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}[Q_{n}(\theta)-Q_{n}(\theta_{0})]<0.$$ 
Note that
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}\left(Q_n(\theta)-Q_n(\theta_0)\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\ln\sigma^2-\ln\sigma^2_0\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left(\ln|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|-\ln|\mathbb{S}_0|\right)-\frac{1}{2n\sigma^2}E\left[\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widetilde{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right]+\frac{c_{u}T-1}{2c_{u}T},\label{Q-Q0}
\end{align}
where $c_u\equiv\frac{n}{NT}$. According to equation (\ref{EVV}), we can decompose equation (\ref{Q-Q0}) into
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{n}\left(Q_n(\theta)-Q_n(\theta_0)\right)=L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)-\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}L_2(\rho,\delta)+o(1),
\end{align*}
where $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\left(\ln\sigma^2-\ln\sigma^2_0\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left(\ln|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|-\ln|\mathbb{S}_0|\right)-\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}(\sigma^2_n-\sigma^2)$ and $L_2(\rho,\delta)\equiv\frac{1}{n}E\{[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)]'\mathbb{Q}[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)]\}$.

%Noted that
%\begin{align*}
%L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)&=\frac{1}{2}\ln\frac{\sigma_0^2}{\sigma^2}+\frac{1}{n}\ln\left|\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right|-\frac{1}{2n}\frac{\sigma_0^2}{\sigma^2}\mathrm{tr}\left[\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\\
%&=-\frac{1}{2}f\left(\frac{\sigma_0^2}{\sigma^2}\left(\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right)'\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\right),
%\end{align*}
%where $f(B)=\frac{1}{n}[\mathrm{tr}(B)-\ln|B|-n]$ for any positive definite $n\times n$ matrix $B$. We have $f(B)\geq0$ and equality holds if and only if all eigen values of $B$ are 1 because $x-\ln x-1\geq0$ and the equality holds if and only if $x=1$, and $\mathrm{tr}(B)$ and $\ln|B|$ are the summation and product of eigen values of $B$.
%Note that $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)$ is exactly the  expected value of the log-likelihood function of a spatial autoregressive model  $\mathbb{Y}=\rho\mathbb{WY+V}$, where $v_{it}\sim \mathrm{N}(0,\sigma_0^2)$. By information inequality of this model, we have $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)\leq0$ and the equality holds if and only if .

For a spatial autoregressive model 
$\mathbb{Y}=\rho_0\mathbb{WY+V}$ with $v_{it}\sim \mathrm{N}(0,\sigma_0^2)$, the information inequality of this model ensures that
$$-\frac{1}{2}\left(\ln\sigma^2-\ln\sigma^2_0\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left(\ln|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|-\ln|\mathbb{S}_0|\right)-\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}(\sigma^2_n-\sigma^2)\leq0.$$
Note that left-hand side of the above equation is precisely $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)$.

Denote $\delta^{(-\gamma)}$ as the vector obtained by removing $\gamma$ from $\delta$, with $\hat{\delta}^{(-\gamma)}$ and $\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0$ defined analogously. By decomposing $\mathbb{Z}\delta$ into $\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}\delta^{(-\gamma)}+ \mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\gamma$, $L_2(\rho,\delta)$ can be viewed as a quadratic function of $\rho$, $\delta^{(-\gamma)}$ and $\gamma$, i.e.,
\begin{align*}
L_2(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)},\gamma)=&\left(\rho-\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}_0\right)E\left[\mathcal{H}^{(-\gamma)}\right]\left(\rho-\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}_0\right)'+\\
&+\frac{2}{n}\left[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(E\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+E\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}(\delta^{(-\gamma)}-\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0)\right]'\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\gamma+\frac{1}{n}\gamma'\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\gamma,
\end{align*}
where $\mathcal{H}^{(-\gamma)}=\frac{1}{n}'(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)})'\mathbb{Q}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0,\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)})$, and $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}=\left((D_1F_1\iota_N)',\cdots,(D_TF_T\iota_N)'\right)'$ is a sparse binary vector with at most $N$ nonzero elements, as we can only observe at most $N$ listed nodes. Therefore, $$\frac{2}{n}\left[(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0(E\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)+E\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}(\delta^{(-\gamma)}-\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0)\right]'\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\gamma+\frac{1}{n}\gamma'\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\gamma=o(1),$$
because elements in $E\mathbb{Z}$ and $E\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}$ are uniformly bounded and $\mathbb{G}_0$ is UB. Thus
$$\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty} L_2(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)},\gamma)=\left(\rho-\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}_0\right)E\left[\mathcal{H}^{(-\gamma)}\right]\left(\rho-\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}_0\right)'\geq0,$$
because $\mathcal{H}^{(-\gamma)}$ is a quadratic form.
%Moreover, $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)\neq0$ as
Below we will show that $(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})$ is globally identified by using either Assumption 8 (i) or Assumption 8 (ii).
\begin{enumerate}
\item (Using Assumption 8 (i))
According to Assumption 8 (i), we have $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty} L_2(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)},\gamma)>0$ whenever $(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})\neq(\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0)$. Combined with $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)\leq0$, it implies that $(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})$ is globally identified.
\item (Using Assumption 8 (ii)) Since $\sigma^2_n$ is uniquely maximizer of $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)$ over $\sigma^2$, the information inequality of the SAR model imples that $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)\leq L_1(\rho,\sigma^2_n)\leq0$. Moreover, $L_1(\rho,\sigma^2_n)=0$ if and only if the pdf $f(\mathbb{Y}|\rho,\sigma^2_n)= f(\mathbb{Y}|\rho_0,\sigma^2_0)$, where
$$f(\mathbb{Y}|\rho,\sigma^2_n)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^\frac{n}{2}\left|\sigma_n^2\mathbb{S}(\rho)^{-1}\mathbb{S}(\rho)^{-1\prime}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2\sigma_n^2}\mathbb{Y'S}(\rho)'\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{Y}\right].$$
If Assumption 8 (ii) holds, $f(\mathbb{Y}|\rho,\sigma^2_n)\neq f(\mathbb{Y}|\rho_0,\sigma^2_0)$ for $\rho\neq\rho_0$ as $n\rightarrow\infty$. Then $\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}L_1(\rho,\sigma^2)<0$ whenever $\rho\neq\rho_0$. Combined with $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty} L_2(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)},\gamma)\geq0$, $\rho_0$ is globally identified. Then $\delta^{(-\gamma)}$ will also be identified because, $$\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty} L_2(\rho_0,\delta^{(-\gamma)},\gamma)=\left(\delta^{(-\gamma)}-\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0\right)'\left[\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}E\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(-\gamma)}\right)\right]\left(\delta^{(-\gamma)}-\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0\right)>0$$
whenever $\delta^{(-\gamma)}\neq\delta^{(-\gamma)}_0$ by Assumption 4.
\end{enumerate}






Once $(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})$ is globally identified, $\sigma^2$ is also globally identified because $\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}L_1(\rho_0,\sigma^2)=\frac{1}{2}(\ln\frac{\sigma_0^2}{\sigma^2}-\frac{\sigma_0^2}{\sigma^2}+1)\leq0$ and the equality holds if and only if $\sigma^2=\sigma^2_0$, because $\ln x-x+1\leq0$ and the equality holds only when $x=1$.
Therefore, $\limsup_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n}\left(Q_n(\theta)-Q_n(\theta_0)\right)<0$ for any $\theta^{(-\gamma)}\neq\theta^{(-\gamma)}_0$, which means $\theta^{(-\gamma)}$ is globally identified.

%Alternatively, if Assumption \ref{asp_Vari} holds, the variance of $\mathbb{Y}$ in the SAR model 
%the determinant of  will differ as long as $\rho\neq\rho_0$,
%$\rho_0$ and $\sigma_0^2$ are globally identified because the information inequality implies that $\lim$


Since $\Theta$ is compact and $Q_n(\theta)$ is uniformly equicontinuous, combined with uniformly convergence and global identification of $\theta^{(-\gamma)}$, we can prove the consistency of $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$. Given $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$, $L_n^c(\theta)$ is maximized at 
\begin{align*}
\hat{\gamma}&=\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{Q\left(S(\hat{\rho})Y-Z^{(-\gamma)}\hat{\delta}^{(-\gamma)}\right)}\\
&=\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{Q}\left(\mathbb{S(\hat{\rho})S}_0^{-1}\left(\mathbb{Z^{(-\gamma)}}\delta_0^{(-\gamma)}+\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}\gamma_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{V}\right)-\mathbb{Z^{(-\gamma)}\hat{\delta}^{(-\gamma)}}\right).
\end{align*}
Since $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\theta_0^{(-\gamma)}$, 
$
\hat{\gamma}=\gamma_0+(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)})^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QV}+o_p(1)$.
Additionally,
$(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)})^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QV}=o_p(1)$ because $E[(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)})^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QV}]=0$ and
\begin{align*}
\mathrm{Var}\left[\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QV}\right]&=\sigma_0^2\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{QZ}^{(\gamma)}\right)^{-1}=\sigma_0^2\left(\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}-\mathbb{Z^{(\gamma)\prime}D}\mathcal{T}^{-1}\mathbb{D'Z}^{(\gamma)}\right)^{-1}\\
&\leq\sigma_0^2\left[(1-c_N)N-\frac{N}{c_TT}\right]^{-1}=O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\end{align*}
where the inequality holds because $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ is a binary vector with at least $(1-c_N)N$ nonzero elements and $T_i\geq c_TT$ by Assumption 1.
Therefore, $\hat{\gamma}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\gamma_0$, so $\hat{\theta}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\theta_0$.

%\mathbb{Q[S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\delta_0-\mathbb{Z\delta+S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{S(\rho)S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}]

\subsection{Proof of Theorem 2}
According to the Taylor expansion
$\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta}=\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}+\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)$, where $\frac{\partial L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta}=0$ and $\bar{\theta}$ lies between $\hat{\theta}$ and $\theta_0$, we further have
\begin{align}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)=\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}-\Delta_n\right),\label{eq_Taylor}
\end{align}
where $\Delta_n=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\varphi_n(\theta_0)+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$, and
$$\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}=\left(\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma^2_0}(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D\alpha}_0)'\mathbb{QV}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\mathbb{V}'\mathbb{G}_0'\mathbb{V}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^*\delta_0)'\mathbb{V}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)\\
&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^{*\prime}\mathbb{V}\\
&-\frac{n}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^2}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{n}\sigma_0^4}\mathbb{V'V}
\end{aligned}\right).$$ 
Since $\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^*=\mathbb{QZ}+\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}^\circ=\mathbb{Z-P(Z-Z}^\circ)$ and $\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{*}=\mathbb{QG}_0\mathbb{Z}+\widetilde{\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}}^{\circ}=\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}-\mathbb{PG}_0(\mathbb{Z-Z}^\circ)$, where
$$Z_t-Z_t^\circ=\left(D_t(I_N-F_t)D_t'M_t(\mathcal{X}_{t-1}\beta_0+\mathcal{E}_{t-1}),D_tD_{t-1}'(\mathcal{X}_{t-1}\beta_0+\mathcal{E}_{t-1}),D_tF_t\iota_N,X_t\right)$$
can be viewed as a constant matrix, elements in $\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}$ follow the form of equation (\ref{eq_clt}). Moreover, since linear combinations of different $\mathcal{Q}_{n}$ still preserve the form of equation (\ref{eq_clt}), and by Lemma \ref{lm_CLT} and Cramér-Wold Theorem, we have 
$$\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}\overset{d}{\rightarrow}N(0,\Sigma_{\theta_0}+\Omega_{\theta_0}),$$
where $\Sigma_{\theta_0}+\Omega_{\theta_0}$ is the asymptotic variance of $\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}$ according to equation (10). Moreover,
\begin{align*}
-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma
=\left[-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)\right]+\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\right)+\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma,
\end{align*}
where the first term is $\|\Gamma^{-1}(\bar{\theta}-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)$ and the second term is $O_p(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}})$ by equations (\ref{eq_H_conti}) and (\ref{eq_H_consi}), respectively. By the consistency of $\hat{\theta}$, $-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma=\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma+o_p(1)$. Moreover, $\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma$ is nonsingular, which has been proved in Appendix \ref{B.3}. So
$\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)=O_p(1)\cdot[O_p(1)+O(\sqrt{N/T})]$ according to equation (\ref{eq_Taylor}), which means that
\begin{align}
\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)=O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right).\label{eq_dtheta}
\end{align}
Hence,
\begin{align*}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)&=\left[\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right)\right]^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}-\Delta_n\right)\\
&=\left[(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right)\right]\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}-\Delta_n\right),
\end{align*}
where the second equality holds by the fact that for any matrix sequences $A_n$ and $B_n$ such that $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}A_n$ exists and nonsingular,  and $B_n$ is $O_p(n^{-\eta})$ for some $\eta>0$, we have
\begin{align}
(A_n+B_n)^{-1}=(I+A_n^{-1}B_n)^{-1}A_n^{-1}=A_n^{-1}-A_n^{-1}B_nA_n^{-1}+A_n^{-1}B_nA_n^{-1}B_nA_n^{-1}-\cdots=A_n^{-1}+O_p(n^{-\eta}).\label{eq_inv_A+B}
\end{align}
Therefore, we have
\begin{align*}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)+\left[(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right)\right]\Delta_n
=\left[(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}+o_p(1)\right]\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}.
\end{align*}
As $\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\Sigma_{\theta_0}$, and $\Delta_n=\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\varphi_n(\theta_0)+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)$, combined with the limiting distribution of $\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Gamma\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}$, we finally have
$$\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)+\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}}\right\}\right)\overset{d}{\rightarrow}N\left(0,\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}(\Sigma_{\theta_0}+\Omega_{\theta_0})\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}\right),$$
which means that
$$\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}\left[\hat{\theta}-\left(\theta_0-\frac{\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}\right)\right]+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}}\right\}\right)\overset{d}{\rightarrow}N\left(0,\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}(\Sigma_{\theta_0}+\Omega_{\theta_0})\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}\right),$$
where one of the multipliers $\Gamma$ can be omitted because the $\gamma$-component of the vector $\varphi_n(\theta_0)$ is zero.
%$\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\partial L_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}-\Delta_n$
\subsection{Proof of Theorem 3}
Since $\hat{\theta}^1=\hat{\theta}+\frac{\hat{B}_n}{c_uT}$, where $\hat{B}_n=\left.\left[\left(-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\right)^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta)\right]\right|_{\theta=\hat{\theta}}$. According to Theorem 2, if $\frac{N}{T^3}\rightarrow0$, we need to prove
\begin{align}
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\Gamma^{-1}\left[\left(-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\right)^{-1}\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})-\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)\right]\overset{p}{\rightarrow}0.\label{eq_B_consi}
\end{align}
Based on equations (\ref{eq_H_conti}) and (\ref{eq_H_consi}), we have
\begin{align*}
-\Gamma\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma&=-\Gamma\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma-\left(-\Gamma\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)+\left(-\Gamma\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)-\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\\
&=\|\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)\|_2\cdot O_p(1)+O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)\\
&=O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right),
\end{align*}
where the last equality holds by equation (\ref{eq_dtheta}). Then using equation (\ref{eq_inv_A+B}), we have
$$\left(-\Gamma\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)^{-1}=(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}+O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right).$$
Recall that $\Gamma^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta)=\varphi_n(\theta)$, thus
\begin{align*}
&\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\Gamma^{-1}\left[\left(-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\right)^{-1}\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})-\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)\right]\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\left[\left(-\frac{1}{n}\Gamma\frac{\partial^2L_n^{c}(\hat{\theta})}{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right)^{-1}\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})-(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)\right]\\
=&\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}(\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma)^{-1}\left[\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})-\varphi_n(\theta_0)\right]+\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})\times O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right)\\
=&O_p\left(\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}\right)\cdot\left[\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta'}\Gamma\right]\left[\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)\right]+\varphi_n(\hat{\theta})\times O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{T},\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}}\right\}\right),
\end{align*}
where $\bar{\theta}$ lies between $\hat{\theta}$ and $\theta_0$. Since $\Gamma^{-1}(\hat{\theta}-\theta_0)=O_p\left(\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right\}\right)$, in order to prove equation (\ref{eq_B_consi}), it suffices to show that $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta'}\Gamma$ is $O_p(1)$.
By exchanging the order of summation in equation (\ref{eq_phi_n}) and adjusting the summation indices, we obtain
\begin{align*}
&\varphi_n(\theta)=\\
&\left(\begin{aligned}
&\left[\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\nu D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\right)+\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbb{G}(\rho)'\mathbb{P})
\end{aligned}\right]\\
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\right)\\
&\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\left(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=T-h}^{T-1}D_{t+1}'D_{t+1}D_{t}'S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\right)\\
&\mathbf{0}_{(k+1)\times1}\\
&\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}
\end{aligned}\right).
\end{align*}
Firstly, we will prove that elements in $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\rho}$ are $O_p(1)$. As
\begin{align*}
&\Bigl|\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\frac{1}{N}\mathrm{tr}\Bigl(
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr)\Bigr|\\
\leq&\frac{1}{N}\Bigl|\mathrm{tr}\Bigl[
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\lambda D_{t+1}'W_{t+1}\Bigl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}S_{t+1}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigr)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr]\Bigr|\\
&+\frac{1}{N}\Bigl|\mathrm{tr}\Bigl[
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}\Bigl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}S_{t}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigr)\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr]\Bigr|\\
&+\frac{1}{N}\Bigl|\mathrm{tr}\Bigl[
\mathcal{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_{t}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr)\Bigr]\Bigr|
\\
\leq&\frac{1}{c_TNT}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1}\mathrm{tr}\Bigl\{
\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[\lambda D_{t+1}'W_{t+1}\Bigl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}S_{t+1}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigr)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_t(\rho)^{-1}\Bigr]
\\&\qquad\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr]\Bigr\}\\
&+\frac{1}{c_TNT}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \mathrm{tr}\Bigl\{\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[
\lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}\Bigl(\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}S_{t}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigr)\Bigr]
\\&\qquad\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr]\Bigr\}\\
&+\frac{1}{c_TNT}\sum_{t=1}^{T-1} \mathrm{tr}\Bigl\{\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[
\lambda D_{t+1}'G_{t+1}(\rho)D_{t+1}
\\&\qquad(I_N-F_{t+1})D_{t+1}'M_{t+1}S_{t}^{-1}(\rho)\Bigr]
\\&\qquad\mathrm{abs}\Bigl[\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}\Bigr]\Bigr\}
\end{align*}
where the last inequality holds because $T_i\geq c_TT$ by Assumption 1. The last equality holds because $W_t$ and $M_t$ are UB uniformly for $t$ by Assumption 5. Additionally, $S_t(\rho)^{-1}$, $G_t(\rho)$, $\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}S^{-1}_t(\rho)=S^{-1}_t(\rho)W_tS^{-1}_t(\rho)$, $\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}$ and $\frac{\partial}{\partial\rho}\sum_{h=0}^{T-t}A_{t}^{(h)}(\theta)D_{t-h}$ are all UB uniformly in a neighborhood of $\rho_0$ for $t$ by Assumptions 5 and 10. Therefore, all the matrices inside the trace operations are UB uniformly for $t$, which means that the derivative above is $O(1)$ in a neighborhood of $\rho_0$.
Since $\hat{\theta}\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\theta_0$, and $\bar{\theta}$ lies between $\theta_0$ and $\hat{\theta}$, $\bar{\theta}$ lies in a neighborhood of $\theta_0$ with probability approaching one. Thus, the derivative above is $O_p(1)$ when evaluated at $\bar{\theta}$.
Using the same approach, we can similarly show that all elements in $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\rho}$, as well as $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\lambda}$ and $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\nu}$ are $O_p(1)$. Since $\sqrt{T}\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\gamma}=\mathbf{0}_{(k+5)\times1}$, $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\beta'}=\mathbf{0}_{(k+5)\times k}$ and $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\sigma^2}=(\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+4)},-\frac{1}{2\bar{\sigma}^4})'$, it follows that elements in $\frac{\partial\varphi_n(\bar{\theta})}{\partial\theta'}\Gamma$ are $O_p(1)$, which completes the proof.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem 4}\label{sec_T4}
Let \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}=(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1},
\widehat{\delta}^{\,1\prime},\widehat{\sigma}^{2,1})'\) be the
bias-corrected estimator. Define
\(
\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
=
(\mathbb D'\mathbb D)^{-1}\mathbb D'
\{\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})\mathbb Y-\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}\}\), and
\(\widehat{\mathbb V}
=
\mathbb Q\{\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})\mathbb Y-\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}\}
\).
Let \(\check\sigma^2=n^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb V}'\widehat{\mathbb V}\),
\(\check\mu_3=n^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^n\widehat{\mathbb V}_r^3\),
\(\check\mu_4=n^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^n\widehat{\mathbb V}_r^4\), and
\(\check c=(\check\mu_4-3\check\sigma^4)/\check\sigma^4\), where
\(\check\sigma^4=(\check\sigma^2)^2\) and
\(\check\sigma^6=(\check\sigma^2)^3\). Let
\(\widehat{\mathbb G}=\mathbb W\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})^{-1}\),
\(\widehat g=(\widehat{\mathbb G}_{11},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbb G}_{nn})'\). Define
\[
\widehat{\Sigma}
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\{\widehat{\mathbb G}(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1})\}'
\mathbb Q
\{\widehat{\mathbb G}(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1})\}
+
\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}\{
\widehat{\mathbb G}(\widehat{\mathbb G}+\widehat{\mathbb G}')
\}
& * & *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\mathbb Q
\widehat{\mathbb G}(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1})
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\mathbb Q\mathbb Z\widetilde{\Gamma}
& *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb G})
&
\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{2\check\sigma^4}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Next, define
\[
\widehat{\Omega}
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{n}\widehat g'\widehat g
+
\frac{2\check\mu_3}{n\check\sigma^4}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb G}\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+
\mathbb Q\widehat{\mathbb G}\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1}
\right]'
\widehat g
& * & *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{\check\mu_3}{n\check\sigma^4}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\widehat g
&
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)}
& *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{2n\check\sigma^2}
\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb G})
&
\displaystyle
\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{4\check\sigma^4}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Set
\[
\widehat{\Xi}
=
\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}
(\widehat{\Sigma}+\widehat{\Omega})
\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}.
\]
\begin{proof}
We first show the consistency of the residual moment estimators. Since
\[
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}
-
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V}
=
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
-(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right],
\]
we need to control the average moments of the right-hand side. For any $n\times n$ UB matrix \(B\) and any vector \(a\), H\"older's inequality implies that, for \(q=2,3,4\),
\begin{align}
\sum_{r=1}^n |(Ba)_r|^q
&=
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\sum_{s=1}^n B_{rs}a_s
\right|^q
\le
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left(
\sum_{s=1}^n |B_{rs}||a_s|
\right)^q
\le
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left(
\sum_{s=1}^n |B_{rs}|
\right)^{q-1}
\sum_{s=1}^n |B_{rs}||a_s|^q
\nonumber\\
&\le
\|B\|_\infty^{q-1}
\sum_{s=1}^n |a_s|^q
\left(
\sum_{r=1}^n |B_{rs}|
\right)
\le
\|B\|_\infty^{q-1}
\|B\|_1
\sum_{s=1}^n |a_s|^q.\label{ineq_UB}
\end{align}
Thus,
\begin{align}
&\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r
-
(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^q\nonumber\\
\le&
\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty^{q-1}\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|\left[
-(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right]_r\right|^q\nonumber
\\
\le&
\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty^{q-1}\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1
(k+4)^{q-1}
\Bigg[
|\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0|^q
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y})_r|^q
+
\sum_{j=1}^{k+3}
|\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}_j-\delta_{0j}|^q
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|\mathbb{Z}_{rj}|^q
\Bigg],\label{eq_V-QV}
\end{align}
where the second inequality holds because
\(
|x_1+\cdots+x_{k+4}|^q
\le
(k+4)^{q-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k+4}|x_j|^q
\).

By the moving-average representation derived in equation (3),
\(
Y_t=\mathcal E_t+\mathcal X_t\beta_0+\mathcal V_t.
\)
The deterministic component \(\mathcal E_t+\mathcal X_t\beta_0\) has uniformly bounded elements by Assumptions~4--6. Moreover, \(\mathcal V_t\) is an infinite historical-error accumulation of the same form as that considered in Lemma~17. Hence, by the same argument as in Lemma~17,
\(
\sup_{i,t,N}E|\mathcal V_{t,i}|^4=O(1).
\)
Therefore, applying Markov's inequality gives
\(
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=1}^n|\mathbb Y_r|^4=O_p(1).
\)
Since \(\mathbb W\) is UB, the equation (\ref{ineq_UB}) gives \[
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=1}^n|(\mathbb W\mathbb Y)_r|^q=O_p(1).
\]
The cases \(q=2,3\) follow from the fourth-moment bound.
Similarly, for each $j$, we obtain 
\[
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=1}^n|\mathbb Z_{rj}|^q=O_p(1).
\]
Together with \(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0=o_p(1)\) and \(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}_j-\delta_{0j}=o_p(1)\) for each \(j\), equation (\ref{eq_V-QV}) gives
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r
-
(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]

Since $\widehat{\mathbb{V}}=\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V}+\widehat{\mathbb{V}}-\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V}$, the binomial expansion gives
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{r=1}^n\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r^q
-
(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r^q
\right|
&\le
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{l=1}^q
\binom{q}{l}
\sum_{r=1}^n|(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r|^{q-l}
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r-(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^l\nonumber\\
&\le \sum_{l=1}^q
\binom{q}{l}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r|^q
\right]^{(q-l)/q}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r-(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^q
\right]^{l/q},\label{ineq_Vh-QV}
\end{align}
where the second inequality follows from H\"older's inequality. Since \(\mathbb{Q}\) is UB, equation \eqref{ineq_UB} gives
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r|^q
\le
\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty^{q-1}\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|\mathbb{V}_r|^q
=
O_p(1),\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
Combining this with
\(
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r
-
(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^q
=
o_p(1),
\)
we obtain
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{V})_r^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]


Next, we show that the sample moments of \(\mathbb Q\mathbb V\) and \(\mathbb V\) are asymptotically equivalent. Since
\(
\mathbb Q\mathbb V
=
\mathbb V-\mathbb P\mathbb V,
\)
it suffices to control the average moments of \(\mathbb P\mathbb V\). Each element of \(\mathbb P\mathbb V\) is an individual-specific average of the errors over the observed periods. For observation \(r\) corresponding to individual \(i\),
\(
(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r
=
\frac{1}{T_i}
\sum_{t:d_{it}=1}v_{it}.
\)
Since the errors are independent with mean zero, variance \(\sigma_0^2\), and fourth moment \(\mu_4\), we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
E|(\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V})_r|^2
=
E\left(
\frac{1}{T_i}
\sum_{t:d_{it}=1}v_{it}
\right)^2=
\frac{1}{T_i^2}
\sum_{t:d_{it}=1}E(v_{it}^2)
=
\frac{\sigma_0^2}{T_i}=O(T^{-1})
\end{aligned}
\]
Similarly,
\[
\begin{aligned}
E|(\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V})_r|^4
&=
E\left(
\frac{1}{T_i}
\sum_{t:d_{it}=1}v_{it}
\right)^4=
\frac{1}{T_i^4}
\left[
\sum_{t:d_{it}=1}E(v_{it}^4)
+
6
\sum_{\substack{s<t\\ d_{is}=d_{it}=1}}
E(v_{is}^2)E(v_{it}^2)
\right] \\
&=
\frac{1}{T_i^4}
\left[
T_i\mu_4
+
3T_i(T_i-1)\sigma_0^4
\right]
=
O(T^{-2}).
\end{aligned}
\]
Moreover, by H\"older's inequality,
\[
E|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^3
\le
\left(E|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^4\right)^{3/4}
=
O(T^{-3/2}).
\]
It follows that, for \(q=2,3,4\),
\(
E|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^q
=
O( T^{-q/2}).
\)
Therefore,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^q
=
O_p(T^{-q/2})
=
o_p(1),
\]
by Markov's inequality.
Moreover, by Assumption~2,
\(
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|\mathbb V_r|^q
=
O_p(1).
\)
Thus, similar to equation \eqref{ineq_Vh-QV}, we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
(\mathbb Q\mathbb V)_r^q-\mathbb V_r^q
\right|
&\le
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{l=1}^q
\binom{q}{l}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|\mathbb V_r|^{q-l}
|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^l
\\
&\le
\sum_{l=1}^q
\binom{q}{l}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|\mathbb V_r|^q
\right]^{(q-l)/q}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\mathbb P\mathbb V)_r|^q
\right]^{l/q}=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Consequently,
\(
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\mathbb Q\mathbb V)_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb V_r^q
=
o_p(1)\), for \(q=2,3,4\), which implies 
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb V_r^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
By the law of large numbers,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb{V}_r^2
\xrightarrow{p}
\sigma_0^2,
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb{V}_r^3
\xrightarrow{p}
\mu_3,
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb{V}_r^4
\xrightarrow{p}
\mu_4.
\]
Therefore,
\[
\check{\sigma}^2\xrightarrow{p}\sigma_0^2,
\qquad
\check{\mu}_3\xrightarrow{p}\mu_3,
\qquad
\check{\mu}_4\xrightarrow{p}\mu_4,
\qquad
\check{c}
\xrightarrow{p}
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{\sigma_0^4}.
\]
We next show that \(\widehat{\Sigma}\) is consistent.
By Theorem~3, we have \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}\xrightarrow{p}\theta_0\). In particular,
\(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0=o_p(1)\).
Since
\(
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})
=
\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)
-
(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W},
\)
applying \eqref{eq_inv_A+B} gives, for \(p=1,\infty\),
\[
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})^{-1}
-
\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)^{-1}
\right\|_p
=
O_p(|\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0|)
=
o_p(1).
\]
Since \(\mathbb{W}\) is UB, for $p=1,\infty$, we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
-
\mathbb{G}_0
\right\|_p
=
\left\|
\mathbb{W}
\left[
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})^{-1}
-
\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)^{-1}
\right]
\right\|_p\le
\|\mathbb{W}\|_p
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})^{-1}
-
\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)^{-1}
\right\|_p
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence, \(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_1=o_p(1)\) and \(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_\infty=o_p(1)\).

From the definition of \(\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}\), we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}-\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
=
\mathbb{P}
\left[
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
\right]-\mathbb{D}\alpha_0=
\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
-
\mathbb{P}
\left[
(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
By Lemma~\ref{lm_VQV}, we have
\(
\frac{1}{n}
(\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V})'\mathbb{P}\mathbb{V}
=
o_p(1).
\)
Moreover, applying the same argument as in \eqref{eq_V-QV} gives
\(
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{P}
\left[
(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right]
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\)
Combining the two bounds above,
we obtain
\[
\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}-\mathbb{D}\alpha_0\|^2=o_p(1).
\]
Since \(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\) and \(\mathbb{G}_0\) are UB,
\(
\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_1=o_p(1)\) and
\(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_\infty=o_p(1)\),
we have
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right\|^2
=o_p(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right\|^2
=o_p(1).\label{eq_GZDGDA}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\]
Moreover,
\(
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
