\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
=
O_p(1).
\)
Therefore,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
\right]
-
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right]
\\
=&
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]
\\
&+
\frac{2}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
For the first term above, we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left|
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]
\right|
\\
\le&
\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty^{1/2}\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1^{1/2}
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
For the second term, by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\left|
\frac{2}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right]
\right|
\\
\le&
2
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
\right]^{1/2}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{Q}
\left\{
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\}
\right\|^2
\right]^{1/2}
\\
\le&
2
\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty^{1/2}\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1^{1/2}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
\right]^{1/2}
\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0)
\right\|^2
\right]^{1/2}
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Hence,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
\right]-
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right]'
\mathbb{Q}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right]
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Similarly,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{Q}
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right)
-
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\mathbb{Q}
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]

Since \(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_1=o_p(1)\), \(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_\infty=o_p(1)\), and $\widehat{\mathbb{G}}$ and $\mathbb{G}_0$ are UB, we have 
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\{
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}+\widehat{\mathbb{G}}')
\}
=
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\{
\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{G}_0+\mathbb{G}_0')
\}
+o_p(1).\label{eq_trGGG}
\end{align}


Since $\check{\sigma}^2\overset{p}{\rightarrow}\sigma_0^2$ and $\sigma_0^2$ is bounded away from zero, the results above imply
\(
\widehat{\Sigma}
-
\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=o_p(1).
\)
Since \(\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Sigma_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\Sigma_{\theta_0}\), we have
\[
\widehat{\Sigma}
\xrightarrow{p}
\Sigma_{\theta_0}.
\]


It remains to show the consistency of \(\widehat{\Omega}\). Similar to \eqref{eq_trGGG}, the same argument gives
\[
\frac{1}{n}\widehat{g}'\widehat{g}
=
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\mathbb{G}_0)_{rr}^2
+o_p(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
=
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)
+o_p(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\widehat{g}
=
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'g_0
+o_p(1),
\]
where \(g_0=((\mathbb{G}_0)_{11},\ldots,(\mathbb{G}_0)_{nn})'\).


Moreover, according to equation \eqref{eq_GZDGDA},
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{Q}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
-
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right\|^2
\\
\le&
\frac{2}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right\|^2
+
\frac{2}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{Q}
\left(
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right)
\right\|^2
\\
\le&
\frac{2}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right\|^2
+
2\|\mathbb{Q}\|_\infty\|\mathbb{Q}\|_1
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right\|^2=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Since
\(
\frac{1}{n}\widehat{g}'\widehat{g}=O_p(1)
\), and \(
\frac{1}{n}g_0'g_0=O_p(1)
\),
the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{Q}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
\right]'
\widehat{g}
-
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{Q}\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
\right]'
g_0
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Combining the above results with
\[
\check{\mu}_3\xrightarrow{p}\mu_3,
\qquad
\check{c}\xrightarrow{p}
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{\sigma_0^4},
\qquad
\check{\sigma}^2\xrightarrow{p}\sigma_0^2,
\]
we obtain
\(
\widehat{\Omega}
-
\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
o_p(1).
\)
Since \(\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Gamma\Omega_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\Omega_{\theta_0}\), it follows that
\[
\widehat{\Omega}
\xrightarrow{p}
\Omega_{\theta_0}.
\]

Finally, Assumption~9 implies that \(\Sigma_{\theta_0}\) is nonsingular. Therefore, \(\widehat{\Sigma}\) is nonsingular with probability approaching one, and by the continuous mapping theorem,
\[
\widehat{\Xi}
=
\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}
(\widehat{\Sigma}+\widehat{\Omega})
\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}
\xrightarrow{p}
\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}
(\Sigma_{\theta_0}+\Omega_{\theta_0})
\Sigma_{\theta_0}^{-1}.
\]
Thus, \(n^{-1}\Gamma\widehat{\Xi}\Gamma\) is a consistent estimator of the asymptotic covariance matrix of \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}\). This completes the proof.
\end{proof}
\subsection{Proof of Theorem 5}
The proof follows the same route as Theorem 1. We first establish the consistency of
$\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$, and then prove the consistency of $\hat{\gamma}$ from its explicit
concentrated expression.

\textbf{Proof of uniform convergence}

The uniform convergence requires showing
\[
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}
\left|
\frac{1}{n}\left(\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)-\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)\right)
\right|
\overset{p}{\to}0,
\]
where $\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)\equiv E[\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)]$. As in the proof of
Theorem 1, it suffices to show
\[
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}
\frac{1}{n}
\left|
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
-
E\left[\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)\right]
\right|
\overset{p}{\to}0,
\]
where
\(
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\equiv
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left(\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{Y}-\mathbb{Z}\delta\right).
\)
Since
\(
\mathbb{Y}
=
\rho_0\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0
+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0
+
\mathbb{V},
\)
and $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{D}=0$, $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{J}=0$, we have
\(
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
=
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left[
\mathbb{V}
-
(\rho-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}(\delta-\delta_0)
\right].
\)
Thus,
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
=&
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{V}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
+
(\rho-\rho_0)^2
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Y}'\mathbb{W}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\frac{1}{n}(\delta-\delta_0)'
\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Z}
(\delta-\delta_0)
\nonumber\\
&-
2(\rho-\rho_0)
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Y}'\mathbb{W}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
-
2\frac{1}{n}
(\delta-\delta_0)'\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
+
2(\rho-\rho_0)
\frac{1}{n}
(\delta-\delta_0)'\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}.
\label{eq_twfe_V'V}
\end{align}
Furthermore,
\[
\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
=
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0+\mathbb{V}
\right).
\]
Compared with Theorem 1, the only additional component is
$\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{J}\tau_0$. Since $\tau_{t0}$ is uniformly bounded by Assumption 4$'$ and
$\mathbb{G}_0$ is UB under Assumptions 3 and 5, this term can be treated in the same way as
$\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0$. Therefore, applying Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ} to each term in
\eqref{eq_twfe_V'V}, all polynomial coefficients in
$n^{-1}\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)$ converge uniformly to their expectations.
Since $\rho$ and $\delta$ are bounded in $\Theta$, we obtain
\[
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}
\frac{1}{n}
\left|
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
-
E\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\right]
\right|
\overset{p}{\to}0.
\]
Because $\sigma^2$ is bounded away from zero, it follows that
\(
\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}
\left|
\frac{1}{n}\left(\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)-\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)\right)
\right|
\overset{p}{\to}0.
\)




\textbf{Proof of equicontinuity}

Next we prove that $\frac{1}{n}\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)$ is uniformly equicontinuous for
$\theta\in\Theta$. The terms involving $-\frac{1}{2}\log\sigma^2$ and
$\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|$ are identical to those in the proof of Theorem~1, and hence
are omitted. Therefore, it remains to verify that replacing the OWFE projection matrix by the
TWFE projection matrix $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}$, together with the presence of the time effects
$\mathbb{J}\tau_0$, preserves the uniform equicontinuity of the normalized expectation term
\(
\frac{1}{n}
E\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\right].
\)

Substituting
\(
\mathbb{Y}
=
\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0+\mathbb{V}
\right)
\)
into $\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)$ gives
\begin{align*}
&E\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\right]
\\
=&
E
\Bigl[
\Bigl\{
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
-\mathbb{Z}\delta
\Bigr\}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\Bigl\{
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
-\mathbb{Z}\delta
\Bigr\}
\Bigr]
\\
&+
E
\Bigl[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}
\Bigr]+
2E
\Bigl[
\Bigl\{
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
-\mathbb{Z}\delta
\Bigr\}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}\mathbb{V}
\Bigr].
\end{align*}
By Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ} and Assumption~1$'$, the last term is
$o(n)$. Moreover, \(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
=
I_n+(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0,
\)
and $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{D}=0$, $\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{J}=0$. Therefore,
\begin{align}
&\frac{1}{n}
E\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\right]\nonumber
\\
=&
\frac{(\rho_0-\rho)^2}{n}
E
\Bigl[
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
\right\}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
\right\}
\Bigr]
\nonumber\\
&+
\frac{1}{n}
E
\left[
(\delta_0-\delta)'
\mathbb{Z}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}
(\delta_0-\delta)
\right]+
\frac{2(\rho_0-\rho)}{n}
E
\Bigl[
\left\{
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right)
\right\}'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}
(\delta_0-\delta)
\Bigr]
+
\widebar{\sigma}_n^2
+
o(1),
\label{eq_twfe_equicontinuity_EVV}
\end{align}
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
\widebar{\sigma}_n^2
=
\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]=
\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]
-
\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
It remains to show that the second term is negligible. Under Assumptions~3 and 5,
\(\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}\) is UB uniformly in \(\rho\in\Lambda\). Since
\(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\) is an orthogonal projection matrix with rank \(N+T-1\), we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left|
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]
\right|
\leq&
\operatorname{rank}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]
\left\|
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right\|_2
\\
\leq&
\operatorname{rank}\left(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right\|_2^2
\leq
\operatorname{rank}\left(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right\|_1
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right\|_\infty
=
O(N+T).
\end{aligned}
\]
The first inequality uses the rank--spectral norm bound for the trace. The second inequality follows from
\(
\operatorname{rank}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]
\leq
\operatorname{rank}\left(\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
\)
and \(\|\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\|_2=1\).
Consequently,
\[
\widebar{\sigma}_n^2
=
\frac{\sigma_0^2}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right)'
\mathbb{S}(\rho)\mathbb{S}_0^{-1}
\right]
+
o(1),\]
uniformly in \(\rho\in\Lambda\). The leading term is the same as the corresponding term in
Theorem~1 and is therefore uniformly equicontinuous in \(\rho\). Hence,
\(\widebar{\sigma}_n^2\) is uniformly equicontinuous.

It remains to show that the first three terms on the right-hand side of
\eqref{eq_twfe_equicontinuity_EVV} are uniformly equicontinuous. These three terms can be
combined as
\(
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta'-\delta_0'
\right)
E\left[\widebar{\mathcal{H}}\right]
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta'-\delta_0'
\right)',
\)
where
\(
\widebar{\mathcal{H}}
\equiv\allowbreak
\frac{1}{n}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right),
\mathbb{Z}
\right)'\allowbreak
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left(
\mathbb{G}_0
\left(
\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0
\right),
\mathbb{Z}
\right).
\)
Under Assumption~4$'$, the elements of the regressors, \(\alpha_0\), and \(\tau_0\) are
uniformly bounded. Together with Assumptions~5--7 and Lemma~\ref{lem:twfe-ZV-ZZ}, we have
\(
E\left[\widebar{\mathcal{H}}\right]=O(1).
\)
Therefore,
\(
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta'-\delta_0'
\right)
E\left[\widebar{\mathcal{H}}\right]
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta'-\delta_0'
\right)'
\)
is a quadratic function of \((\rho,\delta)\) with uniformly bounded coefficients. Therefore it is uniformly equicontinuous.

Combining these results, we obtain that
\(
\frac{1}{n}
E\left[
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)'
\widebar{\mathbb{V}}(\zeta)
\right]
\)
is uniformly equicontinuous for \(\zeta\). Finally, since \(\sigma^2\) is bounded away from zero, multiplying the normalized expectation term by \(-1/(2\sigma^2)\) preserves
uniform equicontinuity. Together with the same treatment of
\(-\frac{1}{2}\log\sigma^2\) and \(\frac{1}{n}\log|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|\) as in Theorem~1, this
implies that
\(
\frac{1}{n}\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)
\)
is uniformly equicontinuous.




\textbf{Proof of global identification condition}

We now prove that for any $\theta^{(-\gamma)}\neq\theta_0^{(-\gamma)}$,
\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)-\widebar{Q}_n(\theta_0)
\right]
<0.
\]
By \eqref{eq_twfe_equicontinuity_EVV}, as in equation \eqref{Q-Q0} in the proof of Theorem 1, we can write
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widebar{Q}_n(\theta)-\widebar{Q}_n(\theta_0)
\right]
=
\widebar{L}_1(\rho,\sigma^2)
-
\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}
\widebar{L}_2(\rho,\delta)
+
o(1),
\]
where
\[
\widebar{L}_1(\rho,\sigma^2)
\equiv
-\frac{1}{2}\left(\ln\sigma^2-\ln\sigma_0^2\right)
+
\frac{1}{n}
\left(
\ln|\mathbb{S}(\rho)|-\ln|\mathbb{S}_0|
\right)
-
\frac{1}{2\sigma^2}
\left(
\widebar{\sigma}_n^2-\sigma^2
\right),
\]
and
\begin{align*}
\widebar{L}_2(\rho,\delta)
&\equiv
\frac{1}{n}E
\Bigl\{
\bigl[
(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
+
\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)
\bigr]'
\\&\qquad
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl[
(\rho_0-\rho)\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
+
\mathbb{Z}(\delta_0-\delta)
\bigr]
\Bigr\}.
\end{align*}
By the information inequality, the same argument as in Theorem 1 gives
\(
\widebar{L}_1(\rho,\sigma^2)
\leq
\widebar{L}_1(\rho,\widebar{\sigma}_n^2)
\leq
0.
\)
Moreover, the terms involving $\gamma-\gamma_0$ in $\widebar{L}_2(\rho,\delta)$ are asymptotically
negligible after normalization by $n$, because $\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}$ is a sparse binary vector with at
most $N$ nonzero elements. Hence
\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\widebar{L}_2(\rho,\delta)
=
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta_0^{(-\gamma)\prime}
\right)
E\left[\widebar{\mathcal{H}}^{(-\gamma)}\right]
\left(
\rho-\rho_0,\,
\delta^{(-\gamma)\prime}-\delta_0^{(-\gamma)\prime}
\right)'.
\]

If Assumption 8$'$ (i) holds, then
$\lim_{n\to\infty}E\left[\widebar{\mathcal{H}}^{(-\gamma)}\right]$ exists and is nonsingular. Therefore,
$\widebar{L}_2(\rho,\delta)$ is strictly positive whenever
$(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})\neq(\rho_0,\delta_0^{(-\gamma)})$, and
$(\rho_0,\delta_0^{(-\gamma)})$ is globally identified.
If Assumption 8$'$ (ii) holds, the information inequality implies
\(
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\widebar{L}_1(\rho,\sigma^2)<0
\)
whenever $\rho\neq\rho_0$. Thus $\rho_0$ is globally identified. Given $\rho=\rho_0$,
Assumption 4$'$ implies that
\(
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{1}{n}
E\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}
\right)
\)
exists and is nonsingular, so $\delta_0^{(-\gamma)}$ is globally identified. 

Once
$(\rho,\delta^{(-\gamma)})$ is globally identified, $\sigma_0^2$ is identified by the same scalar
information inequality as in Theorem 1. Hence $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$ is consistent.

Finally, given $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}\overset{p}{\to}\theta_0^{(-\gamma)}$, the concentrated estimator
of $\gamma$ is
\begin{align*}
\hat{\gamma}
&=
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left(
\mathbb{S}(\hat{\rho})\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}^{(-\gamma)}\hat{\delta}^{(-\gamma)}
\right)
\\
&=
\gamma_0
+
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
+
o_p(1).
\end{align*}
The second equality follows from the consistency of $\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}$ and the same order bounds
used above. As in the proof of Theorem~1, Assumptions~1$'$ and 4$'$ imply that
\(
\frac{1}{N}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\)
is bounded away from zero and uniformly bounded from above.
Therefore,
\[
E\left[
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
\right]
=0,
\]
and
\[
\mathrm{Var}
\left[
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
\right]
=
\sigma_0^2
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
=
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\]
By Chebyshev's inequality,
\(
\left(
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)}
\right)^{-1}
\mathbb{Z}^{(\gamma)\prime}
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
=
o_p(1).
\)
Thus $\hat{\gamma}\overset{p}{\to}\gamma_0$. Combining this with
$\hat{\theta}^{(-\gamma)}\overset{p}{\to}\theta_0^{(-\gamma)}$, we obtain
\(
\hat{\theta}\overset{p}{\to}\theta_0.
\)




\subsection{Proof of Theorem 6}

The proof follows the same Taylor expansion argument as in the proof of Theorem~2. We only present the parts that differ from the OWFE case.

By Theorem~5, \(\widehat{\theta}\xrightarrow{p}\theta_0\). From the first-order condition
\(\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\widehat{\theta})/\partial\theta=0\), the Taylor expansion around
\(\theta_0\) gives
\[
0
=
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
+
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0),
\]
where \(\bar{\theta}\) lies between \(\widehat{\theta}\) and \(\theta_0\). Hence,
\[
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
=
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
\left(
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
\right).
\]
By the TWFE score decomposition in Section~\ref{secB5},
\[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
=
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
-
\widebar{\Delta}_n.
\]
Moreover, by equation~(\ref{B.10}),
\[
\widebar{\Delta}_n
=
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}
\varphi_n(\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{c_uN}}
\psi_n(\theta_0)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),
\]
where \(c_u=n/(NT)\), \(\varphi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)\), and
\(\psi_n(\theta_0)=O(1)\). Here \(\varphi_n(\theta_0)\) is the OWFE incidental-parameter bias component, while \(\psi_n(\theta_0)\) is the additional bias component generated by the time fixed effects.

For the mean-zero score component, the same martingale difference CLT as in the proof of Theorem~2 applies, with \(\mathbb{Q}\) replaced by \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\). Therefore,
\[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
\xrightarrow{d}
N
\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right),
\]
where
\(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\),
\(
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
=
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\Gamma\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\), and $\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}$ and $\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}$ are defined in equation (\ref{eq_TWFE_VAR}). By equation (\ref{eq_TWFE_VAR}), the corresponding second moment satisfies
\[
E
\left[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\right]
=
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
\Gamma\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right).
\]

For the Hessian matrix, equations~(\ref{B.7}) and (\ref{B.8}) imply
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
=
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)
+
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\bar{\theta}-\theta_0)
\right\|_2O_p(1).
\]
Since \(\widehat{\theta}\xrightarrow{p}\theta_0\), we first have
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
=
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
o_p(1).
\]
Under Assumption~9$'\), \(\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma\) is nonsingular for sufficiently large \(n\), which has been proved in Section \ref{secB5}. Hence,
\[
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
=
\left[
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
o_p(1)
\right]^{-1}
\left[
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
-
\widebar{\Delta}_n
\right].
\]
Since the mean-zero score component is \(O_p(1)\) and
\(
\widebar{\Delta}_n
=
O_p\left(\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}\right)
+
O_p\left(\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}\right),
\)
we obtain
\[
\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
=
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right).
\]
Therefore, by (\ref{B.7}) and (\ref{B.8}),
\[
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
=
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right).
\]
Using the standard inverse expansion, we have
\begin{align}
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\bar{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
=
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right).\label{eq_invH_}
\end{align}
Combining the above results gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
=&
\left[
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right)
\right]
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
\\
&-
\left[
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right)
\right]
\widebar{\Delta}_n.
\end{aligned}
\]
Since
\[
\widebar{\Delta}_n
=
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}
\varphi_n(\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{c_uN}}
\psi_n(\theta_0)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
+
O_p\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right),
\]
we have
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
\varphi_n(\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{c_uN}}
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
\psi_n(\theta_0)
\\
=&
\left[
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
+
o_p(1)
\right]
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial\widebar{L}_n^{c*}(\theta_0)}{\partial\theta}
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},
\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}},
\sqrt{\frac{T}{N^3}}
\right\}
\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Because
\(\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0},
\)
and the mean-zero score component converges to
\(
N\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right),
\)
we obtain
\begin{align*}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
&+
\sqrt{\frac{N}{c_uT}}
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
\varphi_n(\theta_0)
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{c_uN}}
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
\psi_n(\theta_0)
\\
&+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},
\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}},
\sqrt{\frac{T}{N^3}}
\right\}
\right)
\xrightarrow{d}
N
\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\left(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right)
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\right).
\end{align*}
Since the $\gamma$-components of both $\varphi_n(\theta_0)$ and $\psi_n(\theta_0)$ are zero, and $\Gamma$ only differs from the identity matrix in the $\gamma$-component, we have
\(
\Gamma^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)=\varphi_n(\theta_0)
\) and \(
\Gamma^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)=\psi_n(\theta_0).
\)
So equivalently,
\begin{align*}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left[
\widehat{\theta}
-
\left(
\theta_0
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right)
\right]
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},
\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},
\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}},
\sqrt{\frac{T}{N^3}}
\right\}
\right)\\
\xrightarrow{d}
N
\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\left(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right)
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\right).
\end{align*}
Furthermore, by Section~\ref{secB5}, \(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)=O_p(1)\), and
\(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)=O_p(1).
\)
If \(v_{it}\)'s are normally distributed, then
\(\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}=0\), and the asymptotic variance reduces to
\(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}\).
This completes the proof.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem 7}

The proof follows directly from Theorem~6 and the same plug-in argument as in the proof of
Theorem~3. Define the bias-corrected estimator as
\[
\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}
=
\widehat{\theta}
+
\left[
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\right)^{-1}
\left\{
\frac{\varphi_n(\theta)}{c_uT}
+
\frac{\psi_n(\theta)}{c_uN}
\right\}
\right]_{\theta=\widehat{\theta}}.
\]
By \eqref{eq_invH_}, we have
\[
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\Gamma
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\widehat{\theta})}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
=
\left(
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
\right)^{-1}
+
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right).
\]
For the plug-in bias correction term, the proof is the same as in the OWFE case. The continuity of \(\varphi_n(\theta)\) follows from Section~\ref{B.4}, and the continuity of
\(\psi_n(\theta)\) follows similarly. Therefore, by the same mean-value argument as in the OWFE case,
\[
\varphi_n(\widehat{\theta})-\varphi_n(\theta_0)
=
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
\right\|_2O_p(1),
\qquad
\psi_n(\widehat{\theta})-\psi_n(\theta_0)
=
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
\right\|_2O_p(1).
\]
By Theorem~6,
\(
\left\|
\Gamma^{-1}(\widehat{\theta}-\theta_0)
\right\|_2
=
O_p
\left(
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right).
\)
Therefore,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}
-
\widehat{\theta}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\\
=&
\left\{
\left[
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\right)^{-1}
\right]_{\theta=\widehat{\theta}}
-
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}
\right\}
\left[
\frac{\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
+
\frac{\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right]
\\
&+
\left[
\left(
-\frac{1}{n}
\frac{\partial^2\widebar{L}_n^c(\theta)}
{\partial\theta\partial\theta'}
\right)^{-1}
\right]_{\theta=\widehat{\theta}}
\left[
\frac{\varphi_n(\widehat{\theta})-\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
+
\frac{\psi_n(\widehat{\theta})-\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right]
\\
=&
O_p
\left[
\left(
\frac{1}{T}
+
\frac{1}{N}
\right)
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
Multiplying both sides by \(\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}\), we obtain
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left[
\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}
-
\widehat{\theta}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right]
=&
O_p
\left[
\sqrt{NT}
\left(
\frac{1}{T}
+
\frac{1}{N}
\right)
\max
\left\{
\frac{1}{T},
\frac{1}{N}
\right\}
\right]
\\
=&
O_p
\left(
\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}
+
\sqrt{\frac{N}{T^3}}
+
\sqrt{\frac{T}{N^3}}
\right)
=
o_p(1),
\end{aligned}
\]
where the last equality follows from \(N/T^3\to0\) and \(T/N^3\to0\).
Therefore,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left(
\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}-\theta_0
\right)
=&
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left[
\widehat{\theta}
-
\left(
\theta_0
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right)
\right]
+
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
By Theorem~6, if \(N/T^3\to0\) and \(T/N^3\to0\), then
\[
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left[
\widehat{\theta}
-
\left(
\theta_0
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\varphi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uT}
-
\frac{\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}^{-1}\psi_n(\theta_0)}{c_uN}
\right)
\right]
\xrightarrow{d}
N
\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\left(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right)
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\right).
\]
Hence,
\[
\sqrt{n}\Gamma^{-1}
\left(
\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}-\theta_0
\right)
\xrightarrow{d}
N
\left(
0,
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\left(
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}
+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}
\right)
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
\right).
\]
If \(v_{it}\)'s are normally distributed, then
\(\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}=0\), and the asymptotic variance reduces to
\(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}\).
This completes the proof.

\subsection{Proof of Theorem 8}

Let \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}=(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1},
\widehat{\delta}^{\,1\prime},\widehat{\sigma}^{2,1})'\) be the TWFE
bias-corrected estimator. Define
\[(\widehat\alpha^{\,1\prime},\widehat\tau^{\,1\prime})'
=
[(\mathbb D,\mathbb J)'(\mathbb D,\mathbb J)]^{-1}
(\mathbb D,\mathbb J)'
\{\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})\mathbb Y-\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}\},\]
and
\(\widehat{\mathbb V}
=
\widebar{\mathbb Q}
\{\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})\mathbb Y-\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}\}\).
Let \(\check\sigma^2=n^{-1}\widehat{\mathbb V}'\widehat{\mathbb V}\),
\(\check\mu_3=n^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^n\widehat{\mathbb V}_r^3\),
\(\check\mu_4=n^{-1}\sum_{r=1}^n\widehat{\mathbb V}_r^4\), and
\(\check c=(\check\mu_4-3\check\sigma^4)/\check\sigma^4\), where
\(\check\sigma^4=(\check\sigma^2)^2\). Let
\(\widehat{\mathbb G}=\mathbb W\mathbb S(\widehat\rho^{\,1})^{-1}\) and
\(\widehat g=(\widehat{\mathbb G}_{11},\ldots,\widehat{\mathbb G}_{nn})'\).

Define
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\left[\begin{array}{cc}
\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\{\widehat{\mathbb G}(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1}
+\mathbb J\widehat\tau^{\,1})\}'
\widebar{\mathbb Q}
\{\widehat{\mathbb G}(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1}
+\mathbb J\widehat\tau^{\,1})\}
\\\displaystyle+
\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}
\{\widehat{\mathbb G}(\widehat{\mathbb G}+\widehat{\mathbb G}')\}
\end{array}\right]
& * & *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\widebar{\mathbb Q}
\widehat{\mathbb G}
(\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1}
+\mathbb J\widehat\tau^{\,1})
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\widebar{\mathbb Q}\mathbb Z\widetilde{\Gamma}
& *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{n\check\sigma^2}\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb G})
&
\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{1}{2\check\sigma^4}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Next, define
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}}
=
\begin{pmatrix}
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{n}\widehat g'\widehat g
+
\frac{2\check\mu_3}{n\check\sigma^4}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb G}\mathbb Z\widehat\delta^{\,1}
+
\widebar{\mathbb Q}\widehat{\mathbb G}
(\mathbb D\widehat\alpha^{\,1}
+\mathbb J\widehat\tau^{\,1})
\right]'
\widehat g
& * & *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{\check\mu_3}{n\check\sigma^4}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb Z'\widehat g
&
\mathbf{0}_{(k+3)\times(k+3)}
& *
\\[2.2ex]
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{2n\check\sigma^2}
\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb G})
&
\mathbf{0}_{1\times(k+3)}
&
\displaystyle
\frac{\check c}{4\check\sigma^4}
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Set
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Xi}}
=
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}^{-1}
(\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}+\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}})
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}^{-1}.
\]


\begin{proof}
We first show the consistency of the residual moment estimators. Since
\[
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}
-
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}
=
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left[
-(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right],
\]
the same argument as in the proof of Theorem~4, with \(\mathbb{Q}\) replaced by
\(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\), gives
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\left|
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r
-
(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V})_r
\right|^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
Here we use that \(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\) is UB, and the moment bounds for
\(\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}\) and the columns of \(\mathbb{Z}\) are the same as those used in
Theorem~4. Therefore, by the binomial expansion and H\"older's inequality,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V})_r^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]

It remains to compare the sample moments of \(\widebar{\mathbb Q}\mathbb V\)
and \(\mathbb V\). Since
\(\widebar{\mathbb Q}\mathbb V=\mathbb V-\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V\),
it suffices to control the average moments of \(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V\).
Since \(\widebar{\mathbb P}\) is an orthogonal projection matrix with
\(\mathrm{rank}(\widebar{\mathbb P})=N+T-1\), for each observation \(r\),
\[
E\left|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r\right|^2
=
\sigma_0^2(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}.
\]
Moreover, since
\((\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r
=
\sum_{s=1}^n(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rs}\mathbb V_s\), the fourth-moment
formula for a linear combination of independent errors gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
E\left|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r\right|^4
=
(\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4)
\sum_{s=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rs}^4
+
3\sigma_0^4
\left[
\sum_{s=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rs}^2
\right]^2\le
c_4
\left[
\sum_{s=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rs}^2
\right]^2
=
c_4(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}^2,
\end{aligned}
\]
where \(c_4=|\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4|+3\sigma_0^4\). The last equality holds because \(\widebar{\mathbb P}\) is symmetric and idempotent,
\[
\sum_{s=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rs}^2
=
(\widebar{\mathbb P}\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}
=
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}.
\]
By H\"older's inequality,
\[
E\left|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r\right|^3
\le
\left\{
E\left|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r\right|^4
\right\}^{3/4}
\le
c_4^{3/4}(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}^{3/2}.
\]
Since \(0\le(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}\le 1\) and
\[
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb P})_{rr}
=
\mathrm{tr}(\widebar{\mathbb P})
=
N+T-1,
\]
we obtain, for \(q=2,3,4\),
\[
E\left[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r|^q
\right]
\le
c_q\frac{N+T-1}{n}
=
O\left(\frac{1}{T}\right)
+
O\left(\frac{1}{N}\right),
\]
where \(c_2=\sigma_0^2\), \(c_3=c_4^{3/4}\), and
\(c_4=|\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4|+3\sigma_0^4\).
Therefore,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
|(\widebar{\mathbb P}\mathbb V)_r|^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
Applying the same binomial expansion as in the proof of Theorem~4, we obtain
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\widebar{\mathbb Q}\mathbb V)_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb V_r^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_r^q
-
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
\mathbb{V}_r^q
=
o_p(1),
\qquad q=2,3,4.
\]
By the law of large numbers, it follows that
\[
\check{\sigma}^2\xrightarrow{p}\sigma_0^2,
\qquad
\check{\mu}_3\xrightarrow{p}\mu_3,
\qquad
\check{\mu}_4\xrightarrow{p}\mu_4,
\qquad
\check{c}
\xrightarrow{p}
\frac{\mu_4-3\sigma_0^4}{\sigma_0^4}.
\]

We next show that \(\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}\) is consistent. By the consistency of the TWFE bias-corrected estimator, \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}\xrightarrow{p}\theta_0\). In particular, \(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0=o_p(1)\). As shown in the proof of Theorem~4, for \(p=1,\infty\),
\[
\left\|
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})^{-1}
-
\mathbb{S}(\rho_0)^{-1}
\right\|_p
=
o_p(1),
\qquad
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-
\mathbb{G}_0
\right\|_p
=
o_p(1).
\]
From the definition of \((\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1\prime},\widehat{\tau}^{\,1\prime})'\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1}
-
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
=&
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\left[
\mathbb{S}(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1})\mathbb{Y}
-
\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
\right]
-
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\nonumber\\
=&
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}\mathbb{V}
-
\widebar{\mathbb{P}}
\left[
(\widehat{\rho}^{\,1}-\rho_0)\mathbb{W}\mathbb{Y}
+
\mathbb{Z}(\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}-\delta_0)
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
As in the proof of Theorem~4, with \(\mathbb P\) replaced by
\(\widebar{\mathbb P}\), we have
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1}
-
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\]
Since \(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\) and \(\mathbb{G}_0\) are UB, and
\(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_1=o_p(1)\) and
\(\|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}-\mathbb{G}_0\|_\infty=o_p(1)\), we have
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right\|^2
&=
o_p(1),
\nonumber\\
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}+\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right\|^2
&=
o_p(1).
\label{eq:twfe-GZ-fixed-vcov}
\end{align}
Consequently,
\[
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\]
Moreover,
\(
 n^{-1}\|\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)\|^2=O_p(1)
\), because the additional term \(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{J}\tau_0\) is handled in the same way as
\(\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{D}\alpha_0\). Therefore, using the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and the UB property of
\(\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
\right]'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
\right]
\nonumber\\
&-
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right]'
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right]
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Similarly,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}
+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})-
\frac{1}{n}
\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{Z}\delta_0+
\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Also, the trace term satisfies
\begin{align}
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\{
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}}+
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}')
\}
=
\frac{1}{n}
\operatorname{tr}
\{
\mathbb{G}_0(\mathbb{G}_0+
\mathbb{G}_0')
\}
+
o_p(1).
\label{eq:twfe-trGGG-vcov}
\end{align}
Similarly, the plug-in argument used in the proof of Theorem~4 gives
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}
-
\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
o_p(1).
\]
Since \(\lim_{n\to\infty}\Gamma\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}\), we obtain
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}
\xrightarrow{p}
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}.
\]

It remains to show the consistency of \(\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}}\). Similar to
\eqref{eq:twfe-trGGG-vcov},
\[
\frac{1}{n}\widehat{g}'\widehat{g}
=
\frac{1}{n}
\sum_{r=1}^n
(\mathbb{G}_0)_{rr}^2
+
o_p(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
=
\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbb{G}_0)+o_p(1),
\qquad
\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'\widehat{g}
=
\frac{1}{n}\widetilde{\Gamma}'\mathbb{Z}'g_0+o_p(1),
\]
where \(g_0=((\mathbb{G}_0)_{11},\ldots,(\mathbb{G}_0)_{nn})'\). Moreover, by
\eqref{eq:twfe-GZ-fixed-vcov},
\[
\begin{aligned}
&
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
-
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right]
\right\|^2
\nonumber\\
\le&
\frac{2}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
-
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
\right\|^2
+
2\|\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\|_\infty\|\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\|_1
\frac{1}{n}
\left\|
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
-
\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right\|^2
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Since \(n^{-1}\widehat{g}'\widehat{g}=O_p(1)\) and \(n^{-1}g_0'g_0=O_p(1)\), the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mathbb{Z}\widehat{\delta}^{\,1}
+
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
(\mathbb{D}\widehat{\alpha}^{\,1}+
\mathbb{J}\widehat{\tau}^{\,1})
\right]'
\widehat{g}
-
\frac{1}{n}
\left[
\mathbb{G}_0\mathbb{Z}\delta_0
+
\widebar{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_0
(\mathbb{D}\alpha_0+
\mathbb{J}\tau_0)
\right]'
g_0
=
o_p(1).
\end{aligned}
\]
Combining the above results with the consistency of \(\check{\mu}_3\), \(\check{c}\), and \(\check{\sigma}^2\), we obtain
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}}
-
\Gamma\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma
=
o_p(1).
\]
Since \(\lim_{n\to\infty}\Gamma\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0,n}\Gamma=\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}\), it follows that
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}}
\xrightarrow{p}
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0}.
\]

Finally, Assumption~9$'$ implies that \(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}\) is nonsingular. Therefore, \(\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}\) is nonsingular with probability approaching one, and by the continuous mapping theorem,
\[
\widehat{\widebar{\Xi}}
=
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}^{-1}
(\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}+
\widehat{\widebar{\Omega}})
\widehat{\widebar{\Sigma}}^{-1}
\xrightarrow{p}
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}
(\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}+
\widebar{\Omega}_{\theta_0})
\widebar{\Sigma}_{\theta_0}^{-1}.
\]
Thus, \(n^{-1}\Gamma\widehat{\widebar{\Xi}}\Gamma\) is a consistent estimator of the asymptotic covariance matrix of \(\widehat{\theta}^{\,1}\). This completes the proof.
\end{proof}
